This page contains approximately 18 lines of "ghost" text. This is not original writing on this surface, but rather **bleed-through**—ink that has seeped through the paper from the reverse side of the leaf over many centuries. Because this is a mirror image of the writing on the back, and due to the significant fading of the ink, the characters are currently illegible. 

The handwriting appears to be in either **Sharada** <note>the ancient script traditionally used for Sanskrit in the Kashmir region</note> or **Devanagari** <note>the script used for modern Hindi and most printed Sanskrit today</note>.

# Om! Salutations to the Illustrious Lord Ganesha.
I bow to Lord Shiva, who is like the sun <note>original: *Shiva-bhāskaram*</note>, who lifts those submerged in the darkness of delusion using the rays of knowledge. Seated upon the peak of Mount Kailasa <note>the sacred Himalayan mountain regarded as the home of Shiva</note>, the God of gods, the Great Lord <note>Maheshwara</note>, is surrounded by celestial attendants and perfected beings <note>Ganas and Siddhas</note>, and served by assemblies of sages. Seated there with him, the Goddess Parvati, the beloved of her husband, questioned the Supreme Lord for the benefit of the world.

<insert>**The Goddess said:**</insert>
O Lord, God of gods, you who wear the crescent moon in your hair, tell me the supreme knowledge with clear meaning, O Great Lord. Speak of the <term>Swarodaya</term> <note>literally "the rising of the breath"; a system of divination and spiritual practice based on the flow of air through the nostrils</note>, the ultimate secret which is difficult even for sages to obtain. O Lord, you have spoken of this before for mortals of limited intellect; now tell it to me both in detail and concisely, O Supreme Lord.

Explain the science of the five paths <note>referring to the five elements: earth, water, fire, air, and ether</note>, the rising of the breath, vowels, and planets, combined with the signs of the zodiac and the assessment of strength and weakness. Reveal the auspicious and inauspicious through the differences of types and the lords of the planets. Speak of gain and loss, happiness and sorrow, life and death, victory and defeat, and of coming and going, O Lord. Regarding matters of thought, secrets held in the hand <note>original: *mushtigam*, a divination technique used to guess an object hidden in a closed fist</note>, and other doubts...

<margin>
within your [body]
</margin>

...by the connection of the breath in the body, through the power of observing the desired... Name <note>The text cuts off here at the end of the page</note>.

<summary>This is the opening page of a Sanskrit treatise on Swarodaya (the science of breath). It begins with a salutation to Ganesha and Shiva, followed by the Goddess Parvati asking Shiva to reveal the secrets of how breathing patterns can predict future events like victory, death, and daily success.</summary>
<keywords>Swarodaya, Lord Shiva, Parvati, Mount Kailasa, breath science, divination, Yuddhajayarnava, five elements, astrology</keywords>

--- Page 9 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Shiva and Parvati regarding the esoteric systems of divination. Parvati is listing specific mystical diagrams (chakras) she wishes to understand, and Shiva begins his response, promising to reveal the secrets of letters, vowels, and planetary movements.</meta>

<page-num>9</page-num>

...by the division of vowels and the alphabetical order of names;
among these, by the classification of zodiac signs, and the <term>Svarottama</term> <note>literally "the best vowel," a technique for determining power based on the first vowel of a person's name</note> and the <term>Shalaka</term> <note>a grid or "spike" diagram used to calculate astrological obstructions</note> charts.
Through these various diagrams, including the <term>Daka-chakra</term> <note>a specific divinatory grid</note>, one gains the knowledge of life.
Through the <term>Kalachakra</term> <note>the "Wheel of Time," a major diagnostic tool in Tantric astrology</note> and the various divisions of birth horoscopes,
one finds certainty in cases of doubt. Tell me of the <term>Pathachakra</term> <note>the "Road Chart," used for predicting the success of journeys</note>, and likewise the
<term>Ayuchakra</term> <note>the "Life-span Chart"</note>, O Lord of Gods, and the <term>Chhalachakra</term> <note>the "Deception Chart," used to identify hidden enemies or tricks</note>.
Also, explain those known as the <term>Chhatra</term> <note>Parasol</note> and <term>Simhasana</term> <note>Throne</note> diagrams,
as well as the <term>Chapa-chakra</term> <note>the "Bow Chart"</note>, the <term>Khadga-chakra</term> <note>the "Sword Chart"</note>, and the <term>Kalasha</term> <note>the "Water-pot" chart</note>.
Furthermore, speak of the <term>Tarachakra</term> <note>the "Star Chart," based on the 27 Nakshatras</note>, the <term>Kotachakra</term> <note>the "Fortress Chart," used specifically to predict the outcome of sieges and battles</note>, and the <term>Rahuchakra</term> <note>the chart of the shadow planet Rahu</note> in its many forms.
And again, the <term>Garudachakra</term> and the <term>Vishtichakra</term> <note>a chart used to avoid inauspicious "Vishti" or Karana periods</note>, O Lord of Gods. According to the alphabetical order
of names, speak of the <term>Bhadyujaya-chakra</term> <note>the "Victory over Planets" chart</note>, O my Lord, and the understanding of the <term>Horas</term> <note>the astrological hours or half-signs</note>.
<margin>In the purification of the Ashoka-hand</margin>
Tell me of the state of the moon and its specific results, and the rising of names, O God of gods, if you please.

When thus addressed by Parvati, the God of gods, <term>Maheshvara</term> <note>The Great Lord Shiva</note>, spoke.
Pleased by her words, he said: "Listen, O Parvati, to this certain truth."
**The Illustrious Lord said:**
"In proper order, I shall tell you everything clearly.
O my beloved, I shall speak of the vowels, the letters, the planets, and the stars—
the characteristics of all these mystical diagrams, which provide immediate conviction <note>original: *pratyaya-karakam*, meaning "producing proof" or "generating confidence"</note>.
The 'rising of the name' has been declared, combined with ten million lineages. 5
It brings victory in the hair-raising battles between the Gods and the Demons."

<summary>Parvati asks Shiva to explain a long list of specific astrological diagrams (chakras) used for predicting life, war, and success. Shiva agrees, stating that these systems provide immediate and certain proof of their efficacy, even in the great battles of mythology.</summary>
<keywords>Svarodaya, Kotachakra, Kalachakra, divination, Shiva, Parvati, martial astrology, mystical diagrams</keywords>

--- Page 10 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Shiva and Parvati from the Yuddhajayarnava (The Ocean of Victory in War). Following the previous page’s list of various mystical diagrams and astrological charts, Shiva now emphasizes the extreme secrecy of this teaching and begins to explain the metaphysical nature of omens and victory.</meta>

<margin>
Pu.
Ja. Na.
2
</margin>

For the sake of victory, O lover of heroes, this scripture of mine is a great secret—it is the **Ocean of Victory in War** <term>Yuddhajayarnava; a specialized Tantric treatise used by kings and warriors to predict the outcomes of battles</term>. I have never explained this to others in such detail, O Parvati. Out of affection for you, I shall speak the truth that brings conviction. This is a secret more hidden than any secret, <margin>du</margin>
O Goddess, and must be guarded with great effort. O beautiful-faced one, it is the true measure [of success]. One should not reveal the truth without being asked, 
O Parvati. Why, O Lord, does the vision fade through neglect? All auspicious and inauspicious 
signs <note>original: "nimittāni"; the OCR indicates a red pigment smudge here</note> are on one side; on the other side is the mind's focus, 
which brings victory. These signs are 
subtle, devoid of a physical body or senses. They are a radiance within the 
bodies of those who know the fruits of the three times <note>the "three times" (tri-kala) are the past, present, and future</note>. Therefore, whether a sign is auspicious or 
inauspicious, one should contemplate it through this inner radiance. Power and natural disposition, O God, 
[are seen] in enthusiasm and passion. From the sounds of instruments to insults, whether they are flourishing 
or fading—a reversal of omens at the time 
of engagement should be managed by waiting out the time or through atonement 
of the conscious soul. Vowels, colors, and the planets are not merely skillful markers; <margin>sva</margin> 
they are pointed out here as the path for all... who in union <margin>daye</margin>

<summary>Shiva explains that victory depends on both external omens and internal mental focus, describing the Yuddhajayarnava as a highly secret teaching revealed only to Parvati. He introduces the concept of "inner radiance" (tejas) as the key to interpreting subtle signs and timing actions correctly.</summary>
<keywords>Yuddhajayarnava, Shiva and Parvati, omens, inner radiance, three times, divination, victory in war</keywords>

--- Page 11 ---
<meta>This page continues the secret teachings of the Yuddhajayarnava, transitioning from the internal state of the warrior to the external cosmic forces. Shiva begins to map out how the planets, elements, and directions interact to create the "fivefold time" that determines success or failure.</meta>

<gloss>Of these three</gloss>

The nectar of speech is obtained through internal reflections; in this fivefold time, among such people, a person of such radiance is rare. Therefore, I have not shown a full elaboration here, but rather a brief overview <term>dig-darshana; literally "a showing of the directions," meaning a summary or guiding outline</term>. Fire is above, water is below, and the Wind <term>prabhañjana; "the breaker," a poetic name for the wind god Vayu</term> moves sideways. In the middle is the void of space. Within this fivefold system, the Sun, the one who is only a head <note>Rahu, the demon of the ascending lunar node</note>, the Son of the Earth <note>Mars</note>, and the Slow-mover <note>Saturn</note>—these rise toward the south. They are cruel planets <term>krura; malefic planets that bring harsh or difficult results</term> and perform cruel actions. The Teacher of the Immortals <note>Jupiter</note>, the Teacher of the Asuras <note>Venus</note>, the Son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note>, and the Night-maker <note>the Moon</note> rise on the left; they perform both auspicious and inauspicious actions. The Sun is fire; the Lame One <note>Saturn</note> is earth; water is... <note>OCR here is fragmented</note> ...this is the rising of the planets, <insert>O Dear one</insert>. The Moon is water; Venus is fire; the Teacher of the Gods <note>Jupiter</note> is wind. The Earth and the Son of the Moon <note>Mercury</note> rise with a leftward movement, O Dear one. Situated in their own state, between the left and right paths, are two *ghatikas* <note>a unit of time lasting 24 minutes</note> and thirty... <unclear>caṣāstriṃśat</unclear>. This practice is considered separately. The time for a change would be half of that, and half of that again... or even for the Son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note>. For the Moon, it is half; for the Son of Bhrigu <note>Venus</note>, half; and for Mercury and Jupiter, a half of a half. Thus in this fourfold group, the fourfold...

<summary>Shiva explains the elemental and directional associations of the planets, categorizing them into "cruel" (malefic) and "auspicious" (benefic) groups based on their movement and elemental nature. He provides specific time measurements for their influence, emphasizing that this knowledge is a rare "overview" of cosmic laws.</summary>
<keywords>astrology, planets, five elements, malefic planets, benefic planets, Rahu, Saturn, Jupiter, Venus, ghatika, Yuddhajayarnava</keywords>

--- Page 12 ---
<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the Yuddhajayarnava, where Shiva explains the calculation of "Chashakas" (tiny units of time) to the Goddess. While the previous page established the elemental associations of the planets, this page focuses on the practical application of these cycles in divination (Prashna) and the vital importance of the "breath" (Prana) in determining success or failure.</meta>

### Page 3

#### Su. Ja. Na. 3

The rule is remembered thus; in this way, one should understand the five <term>chashakas; a unit of time measuring 24 seconds, or 1/60th of a ghatika</term>. Currently, there are twenty-seven *chashakas* <note>The number 27 likely correlates to the 27 Nakshatras or lunar mansions</note> for the Rule of the Sun above. Thus, half and half, in the twenty-seven half-*chashakas*.

<margin>Su. Ja. Na. 3</margin> <insert>In the same way</insert> In this very manner, the measure for the Moon and the other leading planets is declared by these *chashakas*. The Wind <note>the element Vayu</note> moves sideways; in an inquiry <term>prashna; a branch of astrology where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked</term>, the "Life-glance" <note>original: "jiva-drik"; a specific favorable planetary aspect or internal state</note> is remembered. In the burning <note>the fire element</note>, actions are destroyed, O Dear One. When moving through the middle <note>the central channel or sushumna</note>, being in the middle grants great happiness. In the water element, a middle union is considered to increase growth and lifespan.

The prescribed action should be performed by these five <note>the five elements or five time-units</note>. For entering or departing, or in the union of actions, the Wind is auspicious. In war, for victory, and in pleasure, the Wind is very beautiful <note>very favorable</note>. When moving in the equinox <term>vishuva-chara; referring to the balanced flow between the nostrils or the central path</term>, O Goddess, the result is declared to be mediocre.

Having understood this, the wise man should perform actions as he desires. Whether the breath is in the left, the right, or the middle, one should declare the good or bad results, O Dear One. Where the portion of the Breaths <term>prana; the vital life-force or the flow of breath in the nostrils</term> is found, there the inquiry will bring good fortune. In the opposite case, the reverse happens and the action will be destroyed.

Regarding going or not going, the "Foot of the Breath" <term>pranapada; a specific mathematical point in a horoscope related to the breath</term> provides the answer. Success is achieved when the breath has reached the "foot"; it is not achieved when it has moved to the fruit-less side. In an auspicious union of wealth, the Moon will be in Virgo. Whether in an inquiry or at the moment of birth, this is the truth, the truth, O Excellent One.

<summary>This page details the calculation of Chashakas (time units) and explains how the movement of the breath and elements determines the success of specific activities like war, travel, and romance.</summary>
<keywords>Chashaka, Prana, Vayu, Prashna, elements, divination, Yuddhajayarnava, breath control</keywords>

--- Page 13 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between Shiva (Shankara) and Parvati within the context of Svara Shastra (the science of breath). Following the previous page’s discussion on the elemental timing (chashakas), Shiva now details how the success of any action—from travel to daily chores—depends on the alignment of the breath with the correct planetary and elemental cycles. This page marks the transition to a specific diagnostic diagram called the "Circle for Determining the Fivefold Breath."</meta>

...when another day comes, O Parvati, <note>Referring to the transition between the astrological influence of the Sun and the Moon</note> loss will occur in death, O Lovely-faced One. In the *breath-hour* <term>svara-hora; a period of time governed by the flow of breath through a specific nostril</term>, all tasks are illuminated <note>made successful</note> during travel; if the flow is contrary, loss will occur in the house.

In the Sun <note>the right nostril flow</note>, there are five and the four Vedas <note>a numerical code likely referring to 9 units or a specific 5-4 ratio</note>; then the breath is always present. When both channels <note>the nostrils</note> are empty, the breath moving in the central channel is auspicious. He who knows the science of breath knows everything; all actions are illuminated through it. This "Path of the Five" <note>referring to the five elements: Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Space</note> has been declared to you out of love, O Parvati.

Thus <insert>Now</insert> begins the **Circle for Determining the Fivefold Breath**. Now the placement of the diagram is written:

<margin>

...Shankara <note>a name for Shiva meaning "the Auspicious One"</note> responded. By the division of long and short vowels, and according to the order of the bright and dark fortnights <note>the waxing and waning phases of the moon</note>, tell me, O God, how is the breath of humans born?

<summary>Shiva explains that success in all endeavors depends on the flow of breath, introducing the "Prapanchaka Svara Nirnaya Chakra" (a grid mapping vowels and elements). The Goddess asks for clarification on how these breaths function during different lunar phases.</summary>

<keywords>Svara Shastra, five elements (Pancha-tattva), breath divination, Parvati, Shiva, vowels (long/short), lunar fortnights (shukla/krishna), Prapanchaka Svara Nirnaya Chakra</keywords>

--- Page 14 ---
<meta>This page continues the dialogue between the Goddess (Parvati) and the God (Shiva/Ishvara). On the previous page, they established the "Circle for Determining the Fivefold Breath" and the elemental proportions. Here, Shiva begins the technical explanation of how vowels (svaras) and consonants (varnas) correlate with the five elements and the lunar calendar to allow for divination and spiritual insight.</meta>

<margin>
Battle Victory Ocean <note>original: "Yu. Ja. Rne"; abbreviation for the Yuddhajayarnava</note>
4
5
</margin>

...tell me all of this in brief, O Ender of Time! <insert>**Ishvara spoke:**</insert> Listen, O Goddess, I shall explain as you have requested: the determination of the vowels, O Blessed One, and the arising of the consonants.

The letter **A** <note>original: "A-kara"</note> is first, then **I**, and **U** is the third. The letter **E** is the fourth, O Goddess of the Gods, and **O** is the fifth. These five vowels are proclaimed as those by which the entire world is pervaded.

The elements <term>bhutas; the five great elements: Earth, Water, Fire, Air, and Space</term>, such as Earth and the others, are declared to reside in these for embodied beings. Smell, taste, form <note>original: "rupa", transcribed here as "gayo" (likely gandha) and "rasa"</note>, sound, and touch are remembered in this sequence <note>Each vowel corresponds to an element and its sensory quality: A/Earth/Smell, I/Water/Taste, U/Fire/Sight, E/Air/Touch, O/Space/Sound</note>. These five vowels, O Great Goddess, are the illuminators of all actions. Five long vowels <note>Ā, Ī, Ū, Ai, Au</note> are also proclaimed following the sequence of these short ones.

The consonants, from **Ka** to **Ha**, are declared in the grouping. In the division between the bright <note>waxing</note> and dark <note>waning</note> fortnights, they arise, O Beautiful One. The vowel should be known through the sequence of letters and lunar days in the grouping.

In the bright fortnight, the long vowels are rotated three times along with the accompanying syllables. The lunar days <term>tithis; the 30 lunar phases of a month</term>, starting with the first day <term>Pratipada</term> and ending with the full moon, should be known in order. The *Nandā* <note>The first, sixth, and eleventh lunar days are called "Nandā" (Joyous)</note> and all the other categories are also rotated three times throughout the lunar days.

The vowels **A**, **Ṛ**, **Ḷ**, and **Ḷ** <note>The vocalic liquids</note> are assigned among the consonants **Ḍa**, **Jña**, and **Ṇa**. Among the consonants, the short vowels are rotated three times along with the syllables. The lunar days, starting with the first, are joined with their specific names. For each and every name...

# The Division of Lunar Days and the Power of Vowels

Of the lunar days <term>tithis; the 30 phases of the moon</term>, the third should be considered for the sake of poetic wisdom regarding birth and loss. Through eight divided results, the stages of life are known: the **Child**, the **Maiden**, the **Young Woman**, and then the **Bringer of Peace** <note>original: "vishantakari"; likely referring to the stage of an old woman or the approach of death</note> in due sequence.

Joined with the vowels beginning with **A**, the five lunar days are proclaimed. In that context, regarding the performance of actions and counter-actions, the lunar day can be a destroyer of actions. The first three are for the action itself; here, the calculation of one's own vowels should be made.

Starting from the bright fortnight <note>the waxing phase of the moon</note> of the month of **Chaitra** <note>the first month of the Hindu lunar calendar, occurring in March–April</note>, O Goddess, the arising of the vowels occurs. At the end of seventy-two days <note>original: "dvasaptati"; 72 days is exactly one-fifth of a 360-day lunar year, representing the period ruled by one of the five vowels</note>, the rule of each vowel is proclaimed.

### The Cycle of the Twelve Days

Twelve days and twelve days—starting from Chaitra, O Parvati—are proclaimed for each of the letters <gloss>of the</gloss>, O Great Goddess. This "gross" <note>original: "sthula"; referring to the physical or outer calculation as opposed to subtle internal breath</note> aspect is declared to you. These seven vowels/days occur again <insert>O Goddess, for the five vowels every day</insert> <insert>therefore the result is told</insert>.

In the first, and also the sixth, and likewise on the eleventh lunar day, the **Nanda** <term>Nanda Tithi; the "Joyous" lunar days (1st, 6th, and 11th) which are auspicious for starting new works</term> lunar day occurs. It is linked to the sound of the letter **Sha** <note>original: "sha-kara"</note> as the cycle arises. 

<margin>Bright = <note>refers to the Shukla Paksha or waxing moon</note></margin>

On the second, seventh, and twelfth days, which are the **Bhadra** <term>Bhadra Tithi; the "Lucky" lunar days (2nd, 7th, and 12th)</term> lunar days, one should recognize the arising of the vowel **I**. On the eighth and thirteenth days...

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of Svarodaya (the "Arising of Vowels") from the previous page. While the previous page likely covered the first two vowels (A and I) and their corresponding lunar days (Nanda and Bhadra), this page completes the sequence for the remaining vowels and introduces the transition from the "General Method" to the "Subtle Method" of calculation.</meta>

If the constellation is governed by the Serpent deity <note>original: "sarpa-daivata"; referring to the Nakshatra Ashlesha</note>, then the vowel **I** <note>original: "ikara"</note> sets. This is associated with the Victorious lunar day <term>Jaya tithi; the 3rd, 8th, and 13th days</term> and the planet Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note>.

<margin>Ancestors 3</margin> When the deity of the constellation is the Ancestors <note>original: "Pitri"; referring to the Nakshatra Magha</note>, then the vowel **U** sets. This is associated with the Empty lunar day <term>Rikta tithi; the 4th, 9th, and 14th days</term> and the planet Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note>.

If the constellation is governed by the Vasu deities <note>original: "Vasu-daivata"; referring to the Nakshatra Dhanishta</note>, the vowel **E** sets. Similarly, this is linked to the Full lunar day <term>Purna tithi; the 5th, 10th, and 15th days</term> and the planet Saturn <note>original: "Saura"</note>. If the constellation is governed by the god Yama <note>original: "Yama-daivata"; referring to the Nakshatra Bharani</note>, the vowel **O** sets.

In this manner, the destruction of the vowel **A** occurs when the vowel **I** arises. Likewise, the destruction of **I** occurs when **U** arises. In this same manner, when **U** arises, the vowel **E** dissolves, and when **E** arises, the vowel **O** is established, and when **O** arises, the vowel **Ri** is ordained. Thus, the arising and setting of the vowels have been shown according to the **General Method** <note>original: "sthula-margena"; literally the "gross" or broad path</note> involving the gods, sages, planets, deities, and lunar days. I shall now explain the arrangement <term>Prastara; a mathematical or mystical table</term> through a more subtle distinction.

**O Parvati**, this should be applied everywhere as revealed by the Sun...

The half-measure of a zodiac sign is now explained. The third part of a sign is called the <term>Drekkana<gloss>a 10-degree subdivision</gloss></term>, and the ninth part is called the <term>Navamsha<gloss>a 3°20' subdivision</gloss></term>. The twelfth part, O Great God <note>original: "Mahadeva"; referring to Shiva, the traditional source of this knowledge</note>, is known as the <term>Dwadashamsha<gloss>a 2°30' subdivision</gloss></term>. The thirtieth part is the well-known <term>Trimshamsha<gloss>a 1-degree subdivision</gloss></term>; these are the divisions shown here. The total measure of a sign is declared to be 1,800 arc-minutes <note>original: "khava-ekarūpika-liptā"; a technical way of expressing 1,800 minutes (30 degrees × 60 minutes)</note>. Using these specific proportions, one should calculate the portions of the <term>Hora<gloss>a 15-degree subdivision</gloss></term> and the others. 

With sixty <gloss>12</gloss> and twenty <gloss>20</gloss>, with sixty and thirty <gloss>30</gloss>, and with sixty <margin>18</margin> 
and the remaining units, the portions are obtained by division. In the major divisions of the <term>Hora</term> and others, the characteristics of the "wives" <note>This likely refers to the feminine or receptive qualities of certain astrological degrees or specific lunar mansions</note> are identified <margin>Ro_5 Da</margin> through their signs <gloss>1</gloss>. One should divide the <term>Lagna<gloss>the Ascendant or 1st house</gloss></term>, the fourth house known as the <term>Patala<gloss>literally "the underworld," referring to the Nadir or bottom of the chart</gloss></term>, the seventh house known as <term>Jamitra<gloss>derived from the Greek 'diametros', meaning the house directly opposite the Ascendant</gloss></term>, and the tenth house from the Ascendant. The remainder should be applied to the three-fold calculation. The <term>Hora</term> is calculated by sixty parts, the <term>Drekkana</term> likewise by sixty, and for all even signs, the seven-fold divisions <note>original: "saptāṃśa"</note> and the nine-fold divisions are calculated by sixty and ten. For the odd signs, the twelve-fold and thirty-fold divisions are remembered as having their own specific essence.

in the following way, according to the measure of the five and nine <note>This likely refers to the mathematical proportions used to determine the sub-sectors</note>. 
The lords of the <term>Drekkana<gloss>the third-part subdivision of 10 degrees</gloss></term> have been declared for all the zodiac signs, 
O Parvati. Now, the <term>Navamshas</term> <gloss>no</gloss> <note>The gloss "no" appears to be a scribal correction or bridge to the next line's "shaka," completing the word "Navamshaka"</note> must be understood.
O Goddess, one should count them in order for the signs starting with Aries, 
dividing them into their respective portions.
For Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius <note>original: "Mesha-Kesari-Dhanvina"; the fire signs</note>, the <term>Navamshas</term> begin with Aries.
For Taurus, Virgo, and Capricorn <note>original: "Vrisha-Kanya-Mrigana"; the earth signs</note>, the 
<term>Navamshas</term> begin with Capricorn.
For Libra, Gemini, and Aquarius <note>the air signs</note>, 
the <term>Navamshas</term> begin with Libra.
For Cancer, Scorpio, 
and Pisces <note>the water signs</note>, the <term>Navamshas</term> begin with Cancer.
O Goddess, these subdivisions <note>original: "Navamshaka"</note> are remembered within the <term>Varga<gloss>the system of divisional charts</gloss></term> for a specific purpose. 
At the time of application <note>When interpreting a birth chart</note>, this system provides auspicious results. 
For all the signs, the <term>Dwadashamshas<gloss>the twelfth-part subdivisions of 2.5 degrees each</gloss></term> begin with Aries and follow the sequence of the twelve signs.

<summary>This page explains the calculation of the 'Hora' (half-sign) and 'Navamsha' (ninth-part) divisions, detailing which zodiac signs serve as the starting points for these calculations based on their elemental grouping (fire, earth, air, or water).</summary>
<keywords>Hora, Navamsha, Dwadashamsha, zodiac signs, Varga, Shiva, Parvati, Jyotisha, astrological subdivisions</keywords>

<meta>The text on this page transitions from the specific planetary lords of the ninth-parts (Navamshas) to the general qualities and strengths of the twelve zodiac signs. The "Red pigment smudge" noted in the OCR is likely ritual vermilion (sindoor) often applied to sacred manuscripts.</meta>

The lords <note>original: "Adhipah"</note> of the signs starting with Aries were previously declared; 
of Mars, Jupiter, and Mercury <note>original: "Ku-Guru-Saumyanam"</note>, and the cycle of Venus <note>original: "Shukrasya chakramat"</note>. 
By requesting five by five, with equal brilliance in their own signs, 
these are proclaimed for the even signs <note>original: "Yugmarashishu"</note> by the reversal of the <term>Vimshansha<gloss>a twentieth-part subdivision of a sign used to calculate spiritual and general strength</gloss></term>. 
These six categories <note>original: "Shat-padarthah"</note> have been shown to you in brief, O beautiful one <note>original: "Sundari"</note>. 
The Ram, the Bull, the Pair, the Crab, the Archer, and the Sea-Monster <note>original: "Aja-Gopati-Yugma-Karka-Dhanvi-Mriga"; referring to Aries, Taurus, Gemini, Cancer, Sagittarius, and Capricorn</note>, O beloved, 
are known as the night-signs <note>original: "Dinisamjna" - likely a scribal error for "Nisha-samjna" based on traditional astrology and the subsequent margin note</note>; the rest are considered signs of the day. 
<margin>They are known as night-signs, excluding Gemini, and are remembered as rising from the back <term>Prishtodaya<gloss>signs that rise with their hind-part first</gloss></term>, O beloved.</margin> 
The Maiden, the Pair, the Water-bearer, and the Scales <note>original: "Kanya-Mithuna-Kumbha-Tula"; referring to Virgo, Gemini, Aquarius, and Libra</note>, O woman of excellent complexion <note>original: "Varavarnini"</note>, 
must be known as strong in the <term>Lagna<gloss>the Ascendant or the sign rising on the eastern horizon</gloss></term>; they are proclaimed as human signs. 
<margin>8</margin> The Crab and the Fish <note>original: "Karka-mina"; Cancer and Pisces</note> are dwellers of the water; in the fourth house <note>original: "chaturthe"</note> they are strong. In the seventh, 
the Scorpion <note>original: "Vrischika"</note> is the one; it and its kind are declared as the insects <note>original: "Kitadya"</note>. The Ram, 
the Bull, the Man-Tiger <note>likely referring to the centaur-like Sagittarius</note>, and the Lion <note>original: "Simha"; Leo</note> are situated in the tenth house. 
These quadrupeds <note>original: "Chatushpada"</note> possess directional strength <term>Digbala<gloss>the specific potency a sign or planet gains by being in a particular house/direction</gloss></term> in the zodiac. Human signs 
must be known as strong during the day. In the twilight, the water-dwellers are strong. The insects and the 
quadrupeds <note>original: "Chatush-charana"</note> are strong at night. That sign which is 
joined with its own planetary ruler, or with Mercury or Jupiter, or is 
aspected by them, is known to be strong <note>original: "mabalo"; likely a corruption of "sabalo"</note>. 
The remaining signs are without this strength. The Ram, the Bull, the Sea-Monster...

<summary>This page defines the diurnal/nocturnal nature of the zodiac signs and their "directional strength" (Digbala) based on their position in the houses (1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th). It also categorizes signs as human, quadruped, water-dwelling, or insect-like to determine their potency at different times of day.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discourse on planetary strengths, moving from the basic qualities of the signs to the specific degrees of exaltation, primary strength positions (Moolatrikona), and directional/temporal potencies. It maintains the intimate pedagogical tone where the deity instructs his consort ("O beloved").</meta>

<page-num>90</page-num>

The Sea-Monster, the Crab, the Fish, the Scales <note>original: "Mriga-Karka-Meena-Tula"; referring to Capricorn, Cancer, Pisces, and Libra</note>, and the others are the signs where the Sun and the subsequent planets find their exaltation <term>Uccha<gloss>the highest point of a planet's strength in the zodiac</gloss></term>, arranged by their specific degrees. <gloss>Exaltation at 10 degrees</gloss>

The specific degrees of these exaltations are declared as: ten, three <gloss>3</gloss>, fifteen <gloss>15</gloss>, twenty-eight <gloss>28</gloss>, fifteen <gloss>15</gloss>, and twenty-seven <gloss>27</gloss>. 
The deepest exaltation <note>original: "Paramocchakah"</note> is known to be at twenty degrees. From the point of exaltation, the seventh sign thereafter is the place of debilitation <term>Neecha<gloss>the point where a planet is at its weakest</gloss></term>; this should be determined as previously instructed. A planet in its exaltation possesses full strength <note>original: "Purnabale"</note>, whereas in its debilitation, it is devoid of power.

The <insert>Lion</insert> <note>original: "Simha"; Leo. This word is partially obscured by a red mark, likely ritual vermilion.</note>, the Bull, the Ram, the Maiden, the Scales, and the Pot <note>original: "Simho-Vrisho-Yami-Kanya-Tula-Ghate"; Leo, Taurus, Aries, Virgo, Libra, and Aquarius</note> are proclaimed to be the primary strength positions <term>Trikona<gloss>specifically Moolatrikona, a sign where a planet acts with primary office and vigor, secondary only to exaltation</gloss></term> for the Sun and the other lords, O beloved.

In the first house <note>original: "Lagne"; the Ascendant</note>, Mercury and Jupiter are powerful. In the fourth house, the Moon and Venus <note>original: "Margavau"</note> possess strength. In the seventh house, the Son of the Sun <note>original: "Bhanutanayo"; Saturn</note> is strong. In the tenth house, the Sun and the Son of the Earth <note>original: "Bhumijo"; Mars</note> are powerful, O beautiful one, especially when placed in their own divisions <note>original: "Svavarge"</note>.

The Sun, Jupiter, and Venus are strong during the day. Mercury is powerful during both day and night. The remaining planets possess vigor at night. During the bright fortnight <note>original: "Shuklapakshe"; the waxing moon</note>, the benefic planets are strong. The cruel and malefic planets are endowed with strength during their respective days, months, and years. 

Venus and the Moon are strong when placed in feminine signs; the others are strong when situated in the remaining signs. The Moon is powerful when moving in its northern course <term>Uttarayana<gloss>the six-month period when the Sun/planets move northward from the celestial equator</gloss></term>, as are the Sun and the other planets, O beloved. Those planets in retrograde motion <term>Vakra<gloss>appearing to move backward in the sky</gloss></term> are also to be known as being endowed with great strength, even in the southern course.

<summary>This page details the mathematical and positional strengths of the planets, specifically their degrees of exaltation (Uccha), their primary office signs (Moolatrikona), and their directional (Digbala) and temporal (Kalabala) potencies.</summary>
<keywords>exaltation, debilitation, Moolatrikona, Digbala, planetary strength, northern course, retrograde motion, astrological degrees</keywords>

It maintains the instructional tone of a master astrologer or deity clarifying the rules of the celestial science.</meta>

<margin>10</margin>
The "Slow-Mover" <note>original: "Manda"; Saturn</note>, the "Earth-Born" <note>original: "Bhauma"; Mars</note>, the "Gentle One" <note>original: "Saumya"; Mercury</note>, the "Living Soul" <note>original: "Jiva"; Jupiter</note>, the "Bright One" <note>original: "Sita"; Venus</note>, then the Moon and the Sun <gloss>sh</gloss> <gloss>ka</gloss> <gloss>sh</gloss> are each progressively more powerful in their natural state <term>Naisargika Bala<gloss>the inherent, natural strength of a planet regardless of its position</gloss></term>. According to the Great God <note>original: "Mahadeva"; likely referring to Shiva as the source of this knowledge</note>, this natural strength is the most significant. Additionally, planets possess strengths derived from the day, the season, the month, the zodiac sign, their exaltation point, the year, and the time of birth <term>Shadbala<gloss>the "six-fold strength" system used to mathematically determine a planet's actual power</gloss></term>. 

The Sun and Jupiter find their directional strength <term>Dig-bala<gloss>strength gained by being in a specific cardinal direction; e.g., the Sun is strongest in the East/10th House</gloss></term> in the East; Mercury and Jupiter <gloss>va</gloss> in the North; Venus and Mars in the South; and the Moon and Saturn <note>original: "Viku-bhaudra-indu-stryau"; a coded mnemonic for house positions</note> in the West. 

Now, the friends and the crushed enemies of the "Lords of the Sky" <note>original: "Vihagendra"; the planets</note> are described. This rule must be understood by those who wish to master the calculation of the human lifespan <note>original: "marana-jite"; literally "the conquering of death" through knowledge of its timing</note>. 

For the **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi"</note>, **Saturn** <note>original: "Sauri"</note> and **Venus** <note>original: "Bhrigu"</note> are enemies; **Mars** and **Mercury** <note>This specific text groups Mercury here, though schools vary</note> are friends. For the **Moon** <note>original: "Shashi"</note>, **Jupiter** and **Mercury** <note>original: "Jiva-jna"</note> are friends. For **Venus**, **Mercury** is a friend, while the **Sun** and **Moon** are enemies. For **Saturn**, the **Sun**, **Moon**, and **Mars** are enemies who take away strength. 

The friends are traditionally remembered as follows: 
For the **Sun**: the **Moon**, **Mars**, and **Jupiter**. 
For the **Moon**: the **Sun** and **Mercury**. 
For **Mars**: the **Sun**, **Moon**, and **Jupiter**. 
For **Mercury**: the **Sun** and **Venus**. 
For **Jupiter**: the **Sun**, **Moon**, and **Mars**. 
For **Venus**: **Mercury** and **Saturn**. 
For **Saturn**: **Venus** and **Mercury**. 

Regarding neutrals <term>Sama<gloss>planets that are neither friends nor enemies, exerting a balanced influence</gloss></term>: 
For the **Sun** and **Mercury**: the **Moon**, **Saturn**, **Mars**, **Venus**, and **Saturn** <note>the text repeats Saturn to complete the cycle</note> are considered in their respective turn. For **Saturn** and **Mars**, **Jupiter** is neutral. For **Jupiter**, **Saturn** is neutral. For the "Son of Bhrigu" <note>original: "Mrigujasya"; Venus</note>, **Mars** and **Jupiter** are neutral. For **Saturn**, **Jupiter** is remembered as being neutral.

<summary>This page details the hierarchy of natural planetary strengths and the fundamental relationships (friendship, enmity, and neutrality) between the planets. These relationships are essential for determining the auspicious or inauspicious effects of a planet based on the sign it occupies.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the technical discourse on planetary strengths and natures. The previous page ended while beginning to describe the relationships (friends and enemies) of the planets. This page completes that thought and moves into a comprehensive classification of the planets by their Vedic associations, gender, social class (varna), and roles within a celestial royal court.</meta>

Thus, the "Lords of the Sky" <note>original: "kheta"; planets</note> are like kings, possessing friends, enemies, and neutrals. One must understand their gains <note>original: "labha"</note> based on the zodiac sign, day, month, and year.

Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> is the lord of the *Rigveda*; Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> is the lord of the *Atharvaveda* group; in the scriptures, Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> and Venus <note>original: "Sita"</note> are proclaimed as the lords of the *Yajurveda*. 

The waning Moon, the Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note>, and Saturn <note>original: "Saura"</note> are "malefics" <term>Papa-grahas<gloss>planets traditionally considered to have a harsh, difficult, or purifying influence</gloss></term>. The others are "benefics" <term>Saumya-grahas<gloss>planets traditionally considered auspicious, gentle, or nourishing</gloss></term>. Furthermore, Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> is remembered as a malefic when joined with a malefic, and as a benefic when with a benefic.

Mars <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, the Sun, and Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> are masculine. Mercury <note>original: "Somaja"; literally 'son of the Moon'</note> and Saturn <note>original: "Bhanuja"; literally 'son of the Sun'</note> are neuter. Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> and the Moon are spoken of as feminine.

Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> and Jupiter are the Brahmins <note>the priestly and scholarly class</note>. The Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note> and Mars <note>original: "Bhumija"; 'son of the Earth'</note> are the Kshatriyas <note>the warrior and ruling class</note>. The Moon is a Vaishya <note>the merchant and agricultural class</note>. Mercury is a Shudra <note>the laborer class</note>. Saturn is considered the outcaste <note>original: "satkaraja"; referring to those outside the four-fold varna system</note>.

The Sun and the Moon are the Kings; Mars is the Commander-in-Chief <note>original: "Senapati"</note>, O Dear One. Jupiter and Venus are the two Ministers <note>original: "Mantrinau"</note>; Mercury <note>original: "Somaja"</note> is the Prince. Saturn <note>original: "Suryaputra"</note> is the Servant. Such is the division within the "Planetary Assembly" <term>Graha-mandala<gloss>the arrangement of planets personified as a royal court</gloss></term>.

The Moon, the Sun, Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>, and Jupiter... Venus and Saturn... Thus the Moon, Sun, and Jupiter... Truthful Venus... <note>The text becomes fragmentary at the bottom edge of the folio.</note>

<summary>This page catalogs the inherent qualities of the planets, including their Vedic affiliations, gender, and social status. It famously personifies the solar system as a royal court with the Sun and Moon as royalty and other planets as ministers, soldiers, or servants.</summary>

### Functional Roles and Physical Natures of the Planets

Mercury <note>original: "Shashatmaja"; literally 'Son of the Moon'</note>, the two "Kings" <note>the Sun and Moon</note>, Saturn <note>original: "Arkaputra"; 'Son of the Sun'</note>, and Mars—these are known here in the science of astrology. At the time of a query <term>Prashna<gloss>horary astrology where a chart is cast for the moment a question is asked</gloss></term> or at the moment of birth, a planet manifests its own inherent qualities.

Mars, the Sun, Venus <note>original: "Sita-daitya"</note>, and Saturn are designated as **Travelers** <term>Yayi<gloss>planets associated with movement, marching, or those who attack/approach in mundane astrology</gloss></term>, O Dear One. Jupiter and Mercury are known as **Citizens** <term>Paura<gloss>planets associated with stability, the city, or those who are under siege/defending</gloss></term>. The Moon <note>original: "Shasha"</note> is always remembered as the **Crier** <term>Akranda<gloss>the 'allier' planet that stands behind or calls for assistance</gloss></term>.

The Sun and Mars are square-shaped, pungent <note>original: "katu"</note> in taste, and represent four-legged animals. They are governed by the **Bile** humor <term>Pitta<gloss>one of the three bodily temperaments in Ayurveda, associated with heat and metabolism</gloss></term>. They are powerful at midday and are said to "look into the sky" <note>meaning they possess strength when positioned high in the mid-heaven</note>.

Mercury and Jupiter are sweet in taste, circular in shape, and possess balanced humors <term>Sama-dhatu<gloss>a state where the bodily fluids or 'doshas' are in equilibrium</gloss></term>. They are strong at dawn, gentle <note>original: "saumya"</note> in nature, and represent a state of comfort.

Venus and the Moon dwell in watery places; they are governed by the **Phlegm** humor <term>Shleshma/Kapha<gloss>the bodily temperament associated with moisture, stability, and lubrication</gloss></term>. They are powerful during the afternoon.

Saturn is associated with a hiccup-like breath and a long/tall stature. It is linked to the Sun's northern course <note>original: "Uttarayana"</note>, the night-time, and the **Wind** humor <term>Anila/Vata<gloss>the bodily temperament associated with air, movement, and dryness</gloss></term>.

#### Directional Strengths and Governed Materials

Mars, Rahu, Saturn, and the Moon <note>the text lists "Saurach-chandro"</note> are released from... <note>the text becomes slightly fragmented here regarding directional transitions</note>. Jupiter and Mercury are comfortably situated in the East, West, and North.

When Jupiter is dominant, it governs elephants, gold, and gems. The Sun is associated with pearls <note>original: "ravi-moukti"; this is unusual as pearls are typically Lunar; perhaps referring to specific lustrous stones</note>. Three items are specified for Mars. For Saturn, **Iron** is designated. Through the aspect of the Sun, Saturn, and Jupiter, a "bed" <note>original: "khatva"; perhaps referring to furniture or a state of rest</note> is indicated for Mars. The Moon and Venus are considered medium in this regard. The Moon and Mercury... <margin>dhuva 2</margin>

### The Names and Classifications of the Twelve Houses

It is said: Body, Wealth, Sibling, Friend, Son, Enemy, Wife,
Death, Duty, Action, Gain, and Loss <note>original: "tanu-artha-bhrātṛ-suhṛt-putra-ripu-stri-jayā-mṛtyu-dharma-karma-āya-vyaya"</note>—these are the names starting from the Ascendant <term>Lagna<gloss>the point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</gloss></term> in sequence.
The twelve houses should be known. From the Ascendant, the seventh, the fourth, <gloss>2</gloss>
and the tenth are known as the **Angles** <term>Kendra<gloss>the four 'pivots' or cardinal houses of a chart, considered the most powerful locations for planets</gloss></term>. These are the strengths of the signs. <gloss>kāṃ</gloss> <gloss>t</gloss> <margin>ni</margin>
The third, sixth, tenth, and the eleventh (the House of Gains) are of the nature of **Increase** <term>Upachaya<gloss>houses where the influence of planets grows and improves over time</gloss></term>. At the Ascendant
position, O Great Goddess <note>an address to Parvati, the consort of Shiva, who is the traditional recipient of this secret knowledge</note>, all these are for growth. In the ninth <margin>chamaṃ</margin>
position, it is proclaimed as a **Trine** <term>Trikona<gloss>the auspicious 5th and 9th houses, associated with Lakshmi and good fortune</gloss></term>. The fourth house is called the "Abyss" <note>original: "Pātāla"</note>, "Hibuka" <note>original: "Hibuka"; a term derived from the Greek 'hypogeion', meaning the underground point</note>,
the "Home" <note>original: "Veshma"</note>, and the "Body." <note>Note: usually 'Vapu' refers to the 1st house; here it is grouped with the 4th, perhaps referring to the physical foundation</note> The ninth and fifth are the Trines. The "Difficult"
place <note>original: "Ducchikya"</note> is the third house. The "Intelligence" <note>original: "Dhi"</note> place is the fifth.
The seventh house is remembered as the "Diameter" <term>Jamitra<gloss>from the Greek 'diametron', referring to the house directly opposite the Ascendant</gloss></term>. It is also called "Yuna" in calculations <margin>ca</margin>
as it is proclaimed. The eighth is the "Chasm" <note>original: "Chidra"</note>. In the seventh and twelfth is what is called the "Falling" <note>original: "Ripha"; from the Greek 'rhiphe'</note> <gloss>cop</gloss>
likewise in the twelfth. The fourth and eighth houses are proclaimed as the **Square** <term>Chaturasra<gloss>specifically refers to the 'aspect' or positions that create a square geometry in the chart</gloss></term>.
The second, fifth, eighth, and eleventh <gloss>stha</gloss>
houses from the Ascendant are known as the **Succedent** <term>Panaphara<gloss>from the Greek 'epanaphora', houses that 'rise after' the angles</gloss></term>. <gloss>jña</gloss>
The third, sixth, ninth, and twelfth are called the **Cadent** <term>Apoklima<gloss>from the Greek 'apoklima', houses that 'fall away' from the angles</gloss></term>.
The sixth and eighth houses are the "Death-Water" <note>referring to dangerous or vulnerable points in a life map</note> where all planets are considered.
Thus are the names of the Ascendant’s houses. I shall now tell their fruits, O Parvati.
A planet in the House of Gain is auspicious; planets in the Angles or Trines are auspicious.

<summary>This page provides a technical catalog of the twelve astrological houses, listing their Sanskrit and Greek-derived names and grouping them into functional categories like Kendras (angles) and Trikonas (trines).</summary>

</margin>

The malefic houses of increase <term>Upachaya<gloss>the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses where 'malefic' planets are said to provide good results over time</gloss></term> are the best; the opposite ones are considered censured.
A **quarter-gaze** <note>original: "pāda-dṛṣṭi"</note> is declared in the tenth and the third houses; in the ninth and
the fifth, a **half-gaze** <note>original: "ardha-dṛṣṭi"</note> is said, O Fair-Faced One <note>original: "varānane"; an epithet for Parvati</note>. In the fourth and eighth
positions, a **three-quarters gaze** <note>original: "pādone"; literally "a quarter less than full"</note> is proclaimed. In the seventh, it is **full** <note>original: "paripūrnā"</note>.
<margin>॥</margin> Thus it should be calculated. The remaining positions, O God...
the best of planets. In the third and tenth, the Sun <note>original: "Arka"; usually Saturn is credited with the special gaze on 3 and 10, but the text here specifies the Sun</note>
looks and bestows full fruit. In the trines <term>Trikona<gloss>the 5th and 9th houses</gloss></term>,
Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note>; and in the fourth and eighth, Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note>. In the remaining seventh
houses, all planets look with a full gaze. Saturn <note>original: "Sauri"</note> looks fully
at the third and the tenth. In the trines, Jupiter; and at the fourth and eighth, Mars <gloss>॥</gloss>
The Sun <note>The text here says "nārkau," likely "na-arkau," implying a negation or specific condition regarding the Sun</note>, Mercury, and the Moon have a full gaze on the seventh. Just
so, O Great Blessed One, the rule arising from the zodiacal circle has been
shown by me and heard by you, O Beloved. **All**
**verses: 168. The Brahmins whose minds are deluded, [unlike those] who are conquerors of their senses**
**and possessors of steady wisdom, knowers of the meaning of the science of words <term>Shabda-shastra<gloss>grammar and linguistics</gloss></term>, who are**
**free from envy, those devoted to grace and always devoted to the service of the teacher—**
**they do not know [?] other qualities, the best ones, those who hate the scriptures.**
**Thus ends the [section in the] Ocean of Victory in War Tantra <note>original: "Yuddha-jayārṇava-tantra"</note>, in the dialogue between Uma and Maheshvara.**

**The Holy Goddess said:** O Lord, God of Gods, you who wear the crescent moon as your crest, speak to me of the supreme knowledge regarding the movement of the <term>Ascendant<gloss>Lagna; the specific point of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the time of an event</gloss></term>. O Shankara <note>original: "Śaṃkara," a name for Shiva meaning "the Auspicious One"</note>, if you are pleased with me, explain by your grace how to determine good and bad outcomes through every kind of <term>inquiry<gloss>Praśna; the practice of answering questions based on the time they are asked</gloss></term>. 

**The Holy Lord said:** Listen, O Goddess, to this supreme knowledge which is difficult even for the sages to obtain. It is the most excellent of all sciences and is the bestower of <term>liberation<gloss>Mokṣa; the final release from the cycle of reincarnation</gloss></term>. 

In the forenoon, a seeker—even a king—possessing a joyful mind, having performed salutations to the gods and bringing offerings of gold and fruit, should present these to a worthy recipient who knows the science of time. He may then ask whatever he desires to know. The seeker should stand before the <term>astrologer<gloss>Daivajña; literally "the knower of fate"</gloss></term> with humility. 

The astrologer, having received the words of the question from the seeker's mouth, should then reflect upon them. He must determine the Ascendant of that specific moment, considering the <term>six categories of items<gloss>Ṣaṭpadārtha; a technical requirement in divination involving six specific observational factors</gloss></term>. He should calculate the true longitudes of the <term>planets<gloss>Graha</gloss></term> and the <term>zodiac signs<gloss>Rāśi</gloss></term> along with their strength at that specific time, as well as the full power of their <term>aspects<gloss>Dṛṣṭi; the "glance" or mathematical influence planets cast upon other signs</gloss></term>.

<summary>This page establishes the spiritual authority of the text through a dialogue between Shiva and Parvati, outlining the ritual protocol for a seeker to approach an astrologer with a question.</summary>

If [the ascendant] is joined or aspected by <term>Mars<gloss>Kuja</gloss></term> and the <term>Sun<gloss>Arka</gloss></term>, a <term>Mineral or Metal<gloss>Dhatu</gloss></term> query should be declared. Multiply the [longitude of] the ascendant by the [multiplier of] the Sun and other planets, add the [position of] the Moon, and divide by <note>original: "ramaih"; literally "by the Ramas," a traditional numerical code for the number 3</note> three. The remainder indicates the nature of the query: If the remainder is one, it is a <term>Living Being<gloss>Jiva</gloss></term> query. If the remainder is two, it is a <term>Mineral/Metal<gloss>Dhatu</gloss></term> query. If the remainder is zero, it should be known as a <term>Plant/Root<gloss>Mula</gloss></term> query. This is clearly explained in the <italic>Chintamani</italic> <note>A famous classical text on astrological queries</note>.

Then, for the determination of the knowledge of <term>planetary combinations<gloss>yoga</gloss></term> based on the time <gloss>ghatika</gloss> and other factors: in the science of the "hand query" <gloss>hasta-prashna</gloss>, the following materials correspond to the signs:
In <term>Aries<gloss>Mesha</gloss></term>: copper or earth. 
In <term>Taurus<gloss>Vrisha</gloss></term>: musk, or perhaps barley or meat. 
In <term>Gemini<gloss>Mithuna</gloss></term>: roots, flowers, wood, and fruit. 
In <term>Cancer<gloss>Karka</gloss></term>: silver, or things found in pairs.
In <term>Leo<gloss>Simha</gloss></term>: ...

the <term>time units<gloss>ghaṭikā</gloss></term> <note>A ghatika is a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note> are calculated. In a <term>Fixed Sign<gloss>Sthira-lagna</gloss></term>, the object is stationary.

When wealth has departed and is in the hands of a thief, if it is associated with signs of a dual nature, and the stolen property is located in the thief's territory, then if <term>benefic planets<gloss>Saumya</gloss></term> are situated in the <term>angles<gloss>Kendra; the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</gloss></term> or in the <term>increasing houses<gloss>Upachaya; the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</gloss></term>, or likewise in the second house, the owner certainly recovers the lost item. 

The opposite is true for the querent <note>the person asking the question</note> if the conditions are not met. If the full Moon is located in the <term>Ascendant<gloss>Lagna</gloss></term> and is aspected by Mercury or Jupiter, the stolen wealth is recovered. This is also the case if the <term>house of gain<gloss>Labha-bhava; the 11th house</gloss></term> is associated with auspicious planets.

# Calculation for Identifying the Thief

Take the numerical value of the first letter of the name <note>referring to the numerical value assigned to the first syllable of the suspect's or querent's name</note>, multiply it by ten, and add thirty-six to the total. Divide this sum by seventeen, and the remainder is said to describe the thief. Alternatively, one may multiply the first letter of the name by six to identify the thief of the wealth. 

If a companion is involved, the wealth is carried away by them; one should not be afraid among the groups. The appearance, complexion, caste, and excellent qualities of the thief should all be declared based on the influence of the <term>Ascendant<gloss>Lagna</gloss></term>. Furthermore, one should consider the planet that is the lord of the Ascendant and the planet that rules the specific action or nature of the theft.

# Determining the Time of Loss

The knowledge of the time elapsed since the loss is understood through the planets: 
- Through the **Sun**, the time is measured in <term>moments<gloss>muhūrta; approximately 48 minutes</gloss></term>.
- Through the **Moon**, it is measured in days. 
- Through **Mercury**, it is measured in <term>seasons<gloss>ṛtu; two-month periods</gloss></term>. 
- Through **Jupiter**, it is measured in months. 
- The **bright fortnight** <note>the waxing phase of the moon</note> is also declared through these. 
- Through **Saturn** <note>original: "Suryaputra"; literally 'son of the Sun'</note> and the <term>decanates<gloss>drekkana; 10-degree divisions of a zodiac sign</gloss></term>, the entire duration is determined in this way.

# Miscellaneous Rules and the Interpretation of Dreams

If a king, fire, or the color red is involved, or if property is lost at sunrise when the Sun is in the <term>Ascendant<gloss>Lagna</gloss></term> or in the second <term>house<gloss>Bhava</gloss></term>, one should declare the following based on what was seen in a dream. 

If the Moon is the primary influence, one sees a woman, white flowers, various jewels, gold, and coral. 

If <term>Mars<gloss>Kuja</gloss></term> is in the Ascendant, the dream involves meat, traveling through the sky, or perhaps a <term>place of learning<gloss>Pathshala</gloss></term> endowed with <term>righteousness<gloss>Dharma</gloss></term>. In these cases, there are meetings with women and children in the dream-vision. 

When <term>Jupiter<gloss>Guru</gloss></term> or <term>Venus<gloss>Shukra</gloss></term> are influential, the dreamer hears voices or sees visions involving water. If <term>Saturn<gloss>Saure</gloss></term> is present, the dreams constantly involve sexual intercourse, low or base associations, or the climbing of mountains. 

If other planets are in the Ascendant, one should declare mixed dreams. If a <term>malefic planet<gloss>Papa-graha</gloss></term> has gone into <term>debilitation<gloss>Nicha</gloss></term>, or is defeated by an enemy planet, then, <note>original: "deveśi"</note> O Goddess, one should specify that a bad dream <note>literally "duḥsvapna," an inauspicious omen</note> has occurred. 

If the Sun is in the Ascendant or in the seventh house when these dreams are seen:
<margin>Middling</margin> 
...then the result will be of moderate quality. Otherwise, O Goddess praised by heroes, when such an Ascendant is present, one should specify the timing of the dream's result based on the person's current experiences.

In the beginning and in the middle, whatever the truth may be, that should be calculated. Now, regarding the occurrence of five specific weekdays in a month: if there are five Sundays, diseases will break out. <margin>not burning, not</margin> 

The ancient sages say that the results depend on the physical posture of the Transit Personification: 
If the figure is **standing**, it brings a plentiful harvest <note>original: "subhiksha", the opposite of famine</note>. 
If **sitting**, the results are mediocre. 
If **lying down**, there will be <unclear>oppression of the people</unclear>, disease, and indeed famine. 

In the months of <term>Chaitra<gloss>March–April</gloss></term> and <term>Shravana<gloss>July–August</gloss></term>, if there are five occurrences of the same weekday, the following happens: 
- On a **Tuesday**, the King dies. 
- On a **Saturday**, a famine occurs. 
- On a **Wednesday**, the <term>wise or merchant classes<gloss>Budhaih</gloss></term> suffer decline. 
- On a **Friday**, the people shall be prosperous and happy.

In the sacred scriptures, this path is followed to understand the prosperity or power of an enemy. O beautiful lady, who can truly know what the immediate future holds unless they possess the knowledge derived from the observation of the twenty-two calculators? <note>This likely refers to a specific set of variables or phonetic divisions used in this system of divination.</note> 

The zodiac signs <term>Rashis<gloss>the twelve signs of the solar zodiac</gloss></term> are proclaimed, O beautiful one of the gods, as being composed of nine quarters each <term>padas<gloss>each lunar mansion is divided into four quarters or 'feet'; nine of these segments constitute one zodiac sign</gloss></term> of the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras<gloss>the 27 lunar mansions of the moon's path</gloss></term>. This sequence begins with Aries <term>Mesha<gloss>the first sign of the zodiac</gloss></term> and continues until the end of Pisces <term>Meena<gloss>the twelfth sign</gloss></term>.

In those lunar mansions, beginning with *Ashwini* <note>the first of the 27 lunar mansions</note> and ending with *Revati*, the knowledge of the stars is remembered in its proper order. I shall now tell you the names of these transitions, O Parvati, by means of the system known as the Hundred-Step Chart <term>Shatapada<gloss>a 10x10 grid used to map letters of the alphabet to astrological influences</gloss></term> for the sake of attaining Vedic knowledge <gloss>attaining</gloss>.

In that chart, O beloved, one should grasp the letters. Through the combination of the rising [sounds] and the vowels, one should lay out ten horizontal and ten vertical rows. From this, there shall be one hundred chambers <note>original: "kosthaka," referring to the cells or squares in the grid</note> for the knower of the lunar mansions. Starting from the eastern direction, O Goddess of the Gods, one should place the letters in order starting from the first chamber: **A, Va, Ka, Ha, Da** <note>original: "avakahada," a traditional mnemonic system used in Hindu astrology to link the first letter of a person's name to a specific quarter of a lunar mansion</note>.

Then, O beautiful Goddess <note>original: "Surasundari," an address to a female deity, likely Parvati, common in Shiva-Shakti tantric dialogues</note>, when the favorable state is corrupted by a planet, the fourfold outcomes regarding marriage are now told in sequence through the influence of <term>Vedha<gloss>an astrological concept where a planet 'pierces' or aspects a specific point or lunar mansion, affecting its quality</gloss></term>. 

By the piercing of the **Sun**, widowhood occurs. Through the piercing of **Mars**, there is the loss of offspring. Food and drink are similarly affected [by the Moon], and through **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note> comes poverty. Through the piercing of the **Teacher of the Gods** <note>original: "Devacharya," referring to Jupiter</note>, one is always filled with sorrow. If **Venus** performs the piercing, the woman will certainly abandon her husband and become a wandering ascetic. By the piercing of **Saturn**, she will become a famous courtesan. In the case of the **Moon**, a loss of luster or vitality should be understood. 

Now I shall tell you another matter regarding the "Seven-Spike" <note>original: "Sapta-shalaka," a specific astrological grid used to calculate obstacles</note> chart of the Moon. When benefic and malefic planets meet in sequence, two <term>Yogas<gloss>astrological combinations or alignments</gloss></term> known as "Spear" <note>original: "Shula"</note> and "Great Spear" <note>original: "Mahashula"</note> are proclaimed. 

For stable works, for marriage, for entering a new home, and for setting out on a journey, one must not act during a **Spear Yoga**; it should be known that death may result. If one travels fully within these directions, there is a danger to one's life. If married under this influence, widowhood follows; therefore, one should avoid all such timings. The lords of Mars and the others, distributed among the seven groups of seven stars, create afflictions when positioned with their own house-stars in the beginning and so on.

It also introduces the Shula (Spear) Yoga, a dangerous alignment to be avoided for all major life events.</summary>

# The Producer of All Success in All Undertakings: The Great [Formula]
## Kṛ Ro Mśrā Pru Ni Mrai <note>original: "कृ रो म्श्रा प्रु नि म्रै" — these are seed syllables or Beeja Mantras, phonemes used in ritual practice that do not have a literal translation but are believed to embody specific spiritual energies.</note>

<term>Yantra<gloss>a mystical diagram used in tantric traditions for meditation or ritual, often acting as a "machine" or tool to focus divine energy</gloss></term>

<note>The following sections represent syllables arranged along the axes of a red grid diagram, likely used for calculating auspicious timing or for use as a protective talisman.</note>

**Syllables of the Upper Grid:**

- **Top Axis:** Kṛ | Ro | Mśrā | Pru | Ni | Mrai
- **Left Axis (Vertical):** Ma | Pru | Re | U | Pu | Śa | Ya
- **Right Axis (Vertical):** Ma | Pru | U | Ru | Ni | Mrai | Ti

<note>This section describes a second, more complex seven-by-seven grid. The syllables here are prefixed with "Caṃ," a nasalized seed sound often associated with lunar energy or the Bindu (the point of infinite potential).</note>

**Syllables of the Lower Grid:**

- **Outer Top Labels:** Caṃ-ke | Caṃ-me | Caṃ-re | Caṃ-mrā | Caṃ-pu | Caṃ-ni | Caṃ-mrai

<note>The specific combinations of syllables like "Mṛ-tu" (resembling the word for death) and "Vi-gra" (resembling the word for obstacle) suggest this grid is used to map out and avoid specific dangers in a person's horoscope.</note>

<page-num>99</page-num>

<gloss>...ends.</gloss>
<note>original: "ति" (ti). This is the concluding syllable of a word or sentence carried over from the previous page.</note>

In the realm of divine fate, the embodied soul performs its actions. When the moon resides in the third <term>Nakshatra<gloss>lunar mansion</gloss></term> from the <term>Uttara<gloss>the "latter" stars: Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, or Uttara Bhadrapada</gloss></term> group, or in **Pushya**, or **Anuradha**—the star of friendship—it creates a specific alignment. 

If this alignment is afflicted, it brings forth fear, the effects of poison, and the danger of fire. It plagues the embodied soul with disease, grief, and burning pain. This is the certain conclusion regarding the soul's birth star.

Furthermore, the ancient sages have explained the various ways in which "piercing" <note>original: "विद्ध" (viddha). In astrology, this refers to 'Vedha,' where the influence of one planet obstructs or negatively impacts a specific constellation.</note> affects not only humans but also plants, vines, and especially animals. 

When **Ashvini** is pierced by **Jupiter**, it portends the fall of kings and the loss of their status. O beautiful one, such an alignment causes the destruction of all undertakings. 

Similarly, when **Jyeshtha** is obstructed by the Sun's harsh rays, it destroys prosperity. In such times, one should avoid:
*   Marriage ceremonies
*   The sale of livestock

The constellations ruled by Fire, the Terrible one, the Waters, and the World-Deities <note>original: "आग्नेय रौद्र जलभस्वा" (Āgneya Raudra Jalabhasvā). These correspond to Krittika (Agni), Ardra (Rudra), Purva Ashadha (Apah), and Uttara Ashadha (Vishvedevas).</note>, as well as **Abhijit**, also suffer when "pierced," leading to knee pain and general sickness. 

The movement of the Sun through the "Wheel of Fire" <note>original: "आग्नेयादिक भानो चक्र" (Āgneyādika bhāno cakra). A specific astrological diagnostic diagram.</note> must be observed for the satisfaction of the seeker. This fourfold classification and the groupings of seven involve the Sun, Mercury, Saturn, and Rahu in sequence. 

O noble lady, Rahu and Saturn are the primary lords to be considered here. When they mutually pierce one another, the defect <note>original: "दोष" (dosha). A flaw or negative astrological influence.</note> spreads to the directions, the towns, the cities, the villages, and even to one's gold and grains. The "obstruction of the king" is a grave fault that ruins the harvest. Saturn and Rahu, calculated in sets of seven, govern these 
<gloss>stars.</gloss>
<note>original: "भानि च" (bhāni ca). This is a catchword at the bottom of the page, meant to lead into the first word of the following page.</note>

<summary>This page details the negative consequences of "Vedha" (astrological piercing), explaining how specific planetary alignments with lunar mansions cause illness, political ruin, and agricultural failure. It specifically warns against marriage and trade during these inauspicious times.</summary>

<margin>Page 28</margin>
<gloss>ta</gloss> <note>original: "त" (ta). The concluding syllable of a word from the previous page.</note> ...situated in the stars according to their proper order. By these [star positions] and by us, one must proceed toward success. O Goddess who is praised by heroes, one should move either equally or first toward the East; one should travel in the eastern direction. One must not cross the "Iron Bar" <note>original: "परिघ" (parigha). In this context, it refers to a specific malefic astrological period or a symbolic barrier in certain directions that prevents safe travel.</note>, O Goddess, when it is located in the directions of Fire <gloss>Southeast</gloss> or Wind <gloss>Northwest</gloss>. He who crosses it out of delusion, O Parvati, meets his death.

The middle ground between the South and the Southeast is known as the "Enjoyment" <note>original: "भोज" (bhoja)</note> point. Between the constellations of **Vishakha** and **Anuradha** <note>original: "मैत्र" (maitra). Anuradha is ruled by the deity Mitra.</note>, there is an equinoctial point. In that same way, there occurs a "Union of Oceans" <note>original: "समुद्रयोग" (samudra-yoga). A specific astrological configuration where planets are distributed in a way that resembles the vastness or meeting of seas.</note>. 

Through this union, O Goddess, a fierce mutual enmity is produced. Therefore, one should avoid that time. One should not undertake marriages or journeys during this "Union of Oceans." O Goddess, Queen of the Gods, if one travels during this interval, even the rivers and the vines become cruel and harmful. 

One should conduct an examination in the manner previously described to determine the results here. Thus, O illustrious one, this supreme **Spike Diagram** <note>original: "शलाकाचक्र" (shalaka-chakra). A grid-like divination tool used to predict the outcome of events based on planetary 'piercings' of specific lines.</note> has been shown in great detail, organized by the divisions of names.

<meta>Continuity: This page transitions from the general discussion of travel and equinoctial points to a specific technical manual for constructing the "Eighty-One Square Grid" (Ekashiti-pada Chakra). It details how to populate this grid with vowels, constellations, and zodiac signs to predict life events through the system of "piercings" (Vedha) or astrological aspects.</meta>

<page-num>29</page-num>

<margin>Page 29</margin>

...[concluded]. Not in great detail, O Goddess, I shall describe the diagram known as the **Eighty-One Square Grid** as it is taught in the *Science of Breath* <note>original: "Swarodaya" (स्वरोदय). A branch of Vedic astrology and yoga that predicts the future based on the flow of breath and the sounds of names.</note>. This presentation of all matters of interest centers upon the "Spike Diagram" <term>Shalaka Chakra; a grid-based divinatory tool used to calculate the influence of planets on an individual</term> which stands in the middle here, O Parvati. This knowledge provides clarity regarding the three births <note>Refers to the stars associated with one's birth, the previous birth, and the birth of a specific undertaking (Adhana).</note> and friendships.

By drawing ten horizontal lines and ten vertical lines, O Parvati, eighty-one individual squares <gloss>padani</gloss> are created. O Goddess who is praised by heroes, in the corners of this grid, one should place the vowels starting with **"A"** <note>original: "A-kara-adyah svarah". The vowels of the Sanskrit alphabet are considered the 'seeds' of cosmic energy.</note>. 

Then, the constellations starting with **Krittika** <gloss>the Pleiades</gloss> should be placed in the outer rows, beginning from the East. One must also place the twenty numbers of the letters, as well as the consonants beginning with **"Ka"** and **"Da."** Following these, the zodiac signs starting with **Vrishabha** <gloss>Taurus</gloss> are placed in their natural order, along with the lunar days starting with the **Nanda** <note>original: "Nandadi-tithayah". Nanda refers to the 1st, 6th, and 11th days of the lunar fortnight, associated with joy and prosperity.</note> dates. 

This is how the form of the **Eighty-One Square Grid** is constructed. Within it are situated the presiding deities, the constellations, the letters of the alphabet, the zodiac signs, the lunar days, and the planets. 

**The Consequences of Astrological "Piercings"**

If a cruel planet <note>original: "krure". Refers to malefic planets like Mars or Saturn.</note> strikes a square, it causes destruction. If there are two or three such "piercings" <term>Vedha; an astrological 'hit' or aspect where a planet's influence intersects with a specific point in the grid</term>, it is highly inauspicious. 
*   **One piercing:** Causes the loss of one's position or home.
*   **Two piercings:** Leads to a decline in wealth or health.
*   **Double piercing by malefics:** This indicates death. 

When both a benefic and a malefic planet pierce the name of an individual, there is a mix of joy and loss. A triple piercing by malefics causes the end of one's life span. The star of conception <gloss>Adhana</gloss>, the star of birth <gloss>Janma</gloss>, and the star of action <gloss>Karma</gloss> must be protected; if they are struck, it leads to significant misfortune. Even a "good" planet <gloss>Sad-graha</gloss> can yield harsh results if it is placed in a position that causes a conflict with the letters of one's name. 

O consort of Shiva, if the letters of a name are destroyed by these piercings, it affects one's wife, servants, and knowledge. However, if the name-letter is prominent and unaffected, one achieves victory over friends and the destruction of enemies. Greed and delusion vanish when the letters are situated favorably in the four groups of directions, starting with those of **Indra** <gloss>the East</gloss>.

<summary>This page provides instructions for drawing an 81-square astrological grid (Ekashiti-pada Chakra) and populating it with Sanskrit phonemes, constellations, and zodiac signs. It explains the "Vedha" (piercing) system, where the intersection of malefic planets with specific squares predicts loss of status, illness, or death.</summary>
