--- Page 66 ---
<meta>Continuity: This page continues the technical instructions for the Eighty-One Square Grid (Ekashiti-pada Chakra). Having established the grid's layout on the previous page, the text now explains how to interpret the "rising" and "setting" of celestial bodies and how to apply this system to individuals who lack a formal birth chart (horoscope).</meta>

<page-num>30</page-num>

<margin>
Age:
Number
3
</margin>

The **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi" (रवि)</note> is to be known as setting in all positions, while the remaining planets are regarded as rising. Through the constellations, letters, and vowels, one determines the zodiac signs, the lunar days <term>Tithi; a lunar day in the Hindu calendar, calculated by the longitudinal angle between the Sun and Moon</term>, and the directions. Truly, through the brilliance of the Sun, one proceeds for three months. For those being born <gloss>and</gloss> whose birth charts are analyzed, one should take the zodiac sign from the individual's name.

In this **Age of Kali** <note>Kali Yuga: The current era in Hindu cosmology, characterized by a loss of spiritual knowledge.</note>, for those with unknown birth charts <gloss>it should be known</gloss> through the name given at birth. The rising and setting occur in three groups across a period of nine months. In the sky, there are 9 directions, 12 letters, 27 constellations, 14 vowels, and 12 zodiac signs <note>original: "Rashi" (राशि)</note> within the grid. In whichever group the "setting" occurs, the journey <note>original: "Yatra" (यात्रा); refers to both physical travel and the progress of a life undertaking.</note> is considered to be in that direction. Whether combined with auspicious or inauspicious omens, the previous combination results in victory. 

There are fifteen constellations associated with **Saturn** <note>original: "Shani" (शनि)</note> and the letters of the name. In the signs of Cancer <gloss>Karkata</gloss>, the vowels and the Sun are positioned in the four corners. The fifth, ninth, and twelfth are others viewed from above and below. Starting from the star **Magha** <gloss>the Bright One; the star Regulus</gloss>, there are five constellations of Saturn and five letters starting with **"Ma"** (म). The "eyes" <note>This refers to "Drishti" or astrological aspects/glances.</note> fall upon the signs of **Leo, Virgo, and Libra**. Likewise, the second, sixth, tenth, and fourteenth vowels, and the **Bhadra** <note>Bhadra Tithis are specific "fortunate" lunar days (the 2nd, 7th, and 12th of the lunar month).</note> lunar days should be noted. Based on that number in that direction, even if situated there... the five starting with **Maitra**...

Regarding **Scorpio** <note>original: "Keeta" (कीट)</note> and **Sagittarius** <note>original: "Dhanva" (धन्वा)</note>, as well as the **Jaya** lunar day <term>Jaya Tithi: The "Victorious" lunar days (3rd, 8th, and 13th) of the lunar fortnight, usually considered auspicious for success</term>, one should twice consider the constellations of the Varuna group, beginning from **Dhanishtha** and ending with **Yama** <note>Yama refers to the constellation Bharani</note>. 

There are five constellations for **Aquarius**, then **Pisces**, **Aries**, and the [next] three. The **Rikta** lunar day <term>Rikta Tithi: "Empty" days (4th, 9th, and 14th), which are traditionally avoided for important works</term> should be calculated using the numerical values prescribed for Aries and the other signs. 

The **Secondary Planets** <note>original: "Upagraha" (उपग्रह); these are non-luminous, mathematical points or "shadow planets" used in Vedic astrology to predict specific karmic outcomes</note> are certainly determined by the groups of planets. These secondary planets are known by their relation to the position of the **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi" (रवि)</note>. From where the Sun is situated, look at the fifth house; there, the secondary planet named **Vidyunmukha** <note>literally "Lightning-faced"</note> is located. In this same way, the positions are calculated. Similarly, **Ketu** is designated as the eighteenth or twentieth according to the traditional rules.

The twenty-second is named **Vajra** <note>literally "Thunderbolt"</note>, and the twenty-third is **Kampana** <note>literally "Tremor"</note>. Another is proclaimed as **Musala** <note>literally "Pestle"</note>. In whatever place **Rahu**, **Saturn** <note>original: "Saura" (सौर)</note>, or **Mars** <note>original: "Kuja" (कुज)</note> perform their movement, that constellation is said to be "burned," resulting in death due to the influence of the secondary planets.

**Vidyutputra** <note>literally "Son of Lightning"</note> is said to cause destruction, while the constellation **Mula** brings the sudden death of the master or husband. On the tenth day, the fall of Saturn brings harm to the master. **Ketu** is said to cause the loss of wealth, while the point named **Vajra** causes one to be devoted to another man <note>historically implying social scandal or infidelity</note>. 

**Kampa** <gloss>Tremor</gloss> causes the destruction of one's place or home. Like a piercing spear, there is total destruction. Within the thirty lunar days that make up the day and night cycle, the constellations—including the intercalary constellation **Abhijit** <note>The 28th "hidden" nakshatra used in specific types of time-keeping</note>—are...

It is said that whether for a single purpose or for a hundred, the six fulfillments <note>original: "shat-purna" (षट्पूर्ण)</note> are achieved. From the row of constellations, the letter, and the vowels, come loss or grief. When situated in a zodiac sign <term>Rashi: One of the twelve signs of the zodiac, such as Aries or Leo, used as a fundamental coordinate in Vedic astrology</term>, if obstacles fall on the lunar day <term>Tithi: A lunar day in the Vedic calendar; there are thirty tithis in a lunar month</term>, and the "Five" are in descent, there is fear and death may occur. 

When the constellation <note>original: "Nakshatra" (नक्षत्र)</note> rises, there is satisfaction; from the letter <note>original: "Varna" (वर्ण)</note> comes gain; in the vowel <note>original: "Svara" (स्वर)</note> there is happiness; and in the zodiac sign, there is victory. On the lunar day, power is manifested. These five rising factors bring great results. 

From the Sun <note>original: "Bhanu" (भानु)</note> comes grief and anguish. From the Moon <note>original: "Shashanka" (शशांक)</note>, there is always a sense of exhilaration or pride. From the Son of Earth <note>original: "Bhusuta" (भूसुत); the planet Mars</note> comes death and a lack of affection. From Mercury <note>original: "Budha" (बुध)</note>, wisdom is born. Through Jupiter <note>original: "Guru" (गुरु)</note>, one becomes a protector of knowledge. Through Venus <note>original: "Shukra" (शुक्र)</note>, the vital air/vigor is produced. Through Saturn <note>original: "Shani" (शनि)</note>, there is an abundance of disease and a lack of food and drink. One is deluded by the influence of the North Node <note>original: "Rahu" (राहु)</note>. From the South Node <note>original: "Ketu" (केतु)</note> comes the destruction of wealth. 

The six fulfillments follow the sequence of the bright fortnight <term>Shukla Paksha: The waxing phase of the moon, generally considered auspicious for growth and external success</term>. In the long dark fortnight <term>Asita/Krishna Paksha: The waning phase of the moon, often associated with internal work or more difficult omens</term>, the five actions and the "Full" <note>likely referring to Purna-class lunar days</note> are measured by the seasons. 

22 21 25 65 72 46 59

The digit of death <note>original: "mrita-kala" (मृतकला)</note>... Here, among the letters situated in this grid, are the Moon and Venus. O fair-faced one <note>original: "Varanane" (वरानने); a poetic address to the Goddess, typical of Tantric scriptures</note>, the Moon has sixteen digits <term>Kala: A 'digit' or sixteenth part of the moon's visibility; in this context, it also refers to the sixteen Sanskrit vowels</term>. These vowels are the sixteen established digits. If they are satisfied <note>through proper placement or ritual</note>, one finds contentment; otherwise, there is pain in the body. The gross and subtle rising <note>the physical appearance of a planet vs. its phonetic/vibrational influence</note> must be understood with certainty. The consonants and vowels are considered strong according to the fortnights. In the bright fortnight, the bright elements prevail; in the dark fortnight, those arising from the darkness prevail.

The letters *Ka*, *Tha*, *Atha*, and *Ka*, when recited or focused upon, bring about the acquisition of specific knowledge. Then, through the letters *Ya*, *Ka*, *Pa*, *Ma*, and *Da*, the directions—beginning with the East—are determined. When the Sun <note>original: "Ravi" (रवि)</note> is in the sign of Taurus <term>Vrishabha Rashi: The zodiac sign of Taurus</term> and others, the directions are considered to be "blocked" or "measured." The remaining three directions are said to be always "risen" or active. 

In the Northeast <note>original: "Ishanya" (ईशान)</note>, the Lord <note>likely referring to Shiva</note> and the Moon reside; in the South <note>original: "Yama" (यम)</note>, at the peak of the mountain; in the Southwest <note>original: "Nairitya" (नैऋत्य)</note>; in the West <note>original: "Varuna" (वरुण)</note>; in the Northwest <note>original: "Vayavya" (वायव्य)</note>; and similarly, the Moon <note>original: "Soma" (सोम)</note> is situated in the North. 

The constellations <term>Nakshatra: Lunar mansions or star clusters</term>, the letters, the zodiac signs, the lunar days <term>Tithi: A lunar day in the Vedic calendar</term>, and the directions—these are all measured together. Where the Sun <note>original: "Bhanu" (भानु)</note> occupies three zodiac signs, one should first identify the "Birth Constellation" <term>Janmabha: The Nakshatra the Moon was in at the moment of one's birth</term>, as well as the tenth constellation, known as the "Action Constellation" <term>Karmabha: The 10th Nakshatra from the birth Nakshatra, associated with one's work and public life</term>. 

In the twenty-third position <note>the 23rd Nakshatra from the birth star</note>, the teachers declare "half-destruction" <note>original: "vinasharddha" (विनाशार्द्ध)</note>. The twenty-fifth position is known as the "Mental Constellation" <note>original: "Manasa" (मानसं)</note>. If a person's birth factors are preceded by these rising positions, everything may be lost. Through these nine factors, a King is understood. A King is indeed superior through these three: his caste, his country, and his name/title.

Whatever the subject of the constellation may be, one should know its auspicious or inauspicious results. When the birth constellations are afflicted or oppressed <note>original: "paripidite" (परिपीडिते)</note>, it leads to disease, loss of wealth, and conflict. If the Action Constellation <note>Karmabha</note> is afflicted, it results in the failure of one's works or internal division within a group <note>original: "sanghatike" (संघाति के)</note>. This is what occurs in the communal or collective context.

# The Eighty-One Square Diagram

<note>This 9x9 grid, known as the Ekashiti Pada Chakra, is a fundamental tool in Vastu Shastra (sacred architecture) and Muhurta (electional astrology). Each square represents a specific intersection of a lunar mansion (Nakshatra) and a phonetic sound. By plotting the positions of the planets—specifically the Sun and Moon—within this grid, a practitioner can determine which directions and activities are 'struck' by negative influences like Latta.</note>

As established in the scriptures and sacred traditions, the twenty-first constellation... through various means... the grid-lines <term>Shalaka: Intersecting lines drawn across an astrological chart to check for planetary obstructions</term> and diagonal points... misery occurs and inauspicious outcomes flow. Through the piercing of the tooth <term>Danta Vedha: A specific type of astrological affliction or "obstruction" likened to a bite</term>, and various afflictions of Saturn <note>original: "Shani"</note>, death occurs during a "piercing" <term>Vedha: An astrological phenomenon where the position of one planet "strikes" or negatively affects another point, planet, or sound in a chart</term>. Therefore, one should carefully contemplate the different types of Vedha.

The first is the Fang-Piercing <note>original: "Dantra-vedha"</note>; the second is the Grid-Piercing <note>original: "Shalaka-vedha"</note>; the third is that which proceeds from the Ascendant <term>Lagna: The degree of the zodiac rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of birth</term>; there is a fourth type there as well. The fifth is the Secondary Planet <term>Upagraha: Lesser celestial bodies or shadow planets used in Vedic calculation</term>; the sixth arises from the astrological channels <term>Nadi: Symbolic "nerves" or channels that connect specific constellations</term>; and the seventh is the Piercing of Vital Points <term>Marma-vedha: Afflictions to sensitive "vital" degrees of the zodiac or body</term>. These are the seven proclaimed types of Piercing.

A single piercing results in destruction or displacement from one's home <note>original: "Sthanabhransha"</note>. When two piercings occur simultaneously from malefic planets <note>original: "Papa-graha"</note>, fear is indicated. When three piercings are obtained, then death arises. When four piercings affect a person, an untimely death <term>Apamrityu: A death that occurs before one's destined natural lifespan</term> is foretold. Five piercings result in certain death. In the piercing of sounds <term>Svara-vedha: An affliction affecting the phonetic sounds linked to an individual’s name</term>, death occurs. In the piercing of letters <term>Varna-vedha: An affliction to the specific letters assigned to a birth star</term>, calamity is remembered. In the piercing of the lunar day <term>Tithi: A day in the lunar calendar</term>, there is a confusion of memory, and in the zodiac sign <note>original: "Rashi"</note>, there is the destruction of all efforts.

<summary>This page defines the seven types of "Vedha" (astrological piercing) and describes the increasingly dire consequences—ranging from loss of status to untimely death—as the number of simultaneous afflictions increases.</summary>

### Page 19

In this system, a single piercing causes disease, but one who is pierced by five <term>Pancha-viddha: A condition where five different astrological systems or 'wheels' show simultaneous malefic obstruction</term> does not survive. The element of Earth is said to reside in the Wheel of Constellations <note>original: "Riksha-chakra"</note>; Water is remembered in the Wheel of Phonetics <note>original: "Varna-chakra"</note>. Light <note>referring to the element of Fire</note> has gone into the Wheel of Deities <note>original: "Sura-chakra"</note>, and Air resides in the Wheel of the Zodiac <note>original: "Rashi-chakra"</note>.

The Wheel of Lunar Days <note>original: "Tithi-chakra"</note>, the Constellations, and the others comprise the Seven Wheels. These are the five types, O Dear One <note>original: "Priye"; an address to a goddess, indicating this text is a dialogue between Shiva and Parvati</note>; when there is a piercing across five wheels, there is no doubt that death follows. That which is crossed by a malefic planet <note>original: "Krura-graha," such as Saturn or Mars</note> is known by the wise as "burnt" <term>Dagdha: A state where a point in the zodiac loses its auspicious power due to proximity to the Sun or malefic influence</term>.

One should know the "Embraced" state <note>original: "Alingita"</note> when Mars <note>original: "Bhumija," literally 'son of the Earth'</note> is situated in front. Regions abandoned by the Sun are likewise "burnt"; that which is directly in front becomes "marked." To pacify the influence of these forward positions, one must look to the Vital Points <term>Marma: Sensitive degrees in a chart that correspond to vital organs or life force</term>. One should recognize the "Thorn" <note>original: "Kantaka" or "Kataka"</note> in the Ascendant, the eighth, sixth, and fifth houses. The tenth and fourth houses are known as the "Spike" <note>original: "Shalya"</note>. The "Hole" <note>original: "Chidra"</note> may also occur in the seventh house <note>original: "Jamitra"</note>.

</margin>

A piercing in the Vital Point leads to death, as does the "Hole." From the group of [malefic] influences, the "Spike" results in the falling of a weapon, while the "Thorn" brings about the [misery of the] plow <note>likely referring to heavy, unending labor or being 'yoked' to misfortune</note>. Except for the five families, there are four units of affliction in the Phonetic-Piercing <note>original: "Varna-vedha"</note>. In the Donkey-Piercing <note>original: "Khara-vedha"</note>, there are three units; in the Zodiacal-Piercing <note>original: "Rashi-vedha"</note>, two units. In the Lunar Day-Piercing <note>original: "Tithi-vedha"</note>, there is one unit of affliction. As stated, one pierced by five does not live.

The Wheel of Fangs, the Grid, the Ascendant, and the Secondary Planets <note>original: "Upagraha"</note>, along with the piercings of the Channels <note>original: "Nadi"</note> and the Vital Points—these are proclaimed as the Seven Piercings. However, through the power of mantras, through various acts of charity, and through offerings of potent herbs and sacrifices, one can endure and overcome these effects. The three constellations, including the Fire-constellation, situated in the middle region... through piercings by the malefic Mercury <note>original: "Durbudha"</note>... from the eastern region...

### Page 34

It is said that death follows from the constellation of the Moist One <note>original: "Ardra"; the star Betelgeuse</note> onwards, in groups of three. When these are struck by malefic planets, kings and the people of the Middle Country <term>Madhyadesha: The central region of ancient India, traditionally considered the cultural heartland</term> are tormented in sequence. Those dependent on the ancestors <note>referring to the constellation Magha, whose presiding deities are the Pitris/Ancestors</note> and those in the constellations starting from the Cutters <note>original: "Krittika"; the Pleiades</note>—including the regions of the Five-Lands <note>original: "Panchala"</note>, the Shaudraveyas, and the Great Kingdom <note>original: "Magadha"</note>—suffer. 

The King of the Kalingas <note>an ancient region on the eastern coast of India</note> is affected when the Noble One <note>original: "Aryaman"; refers to the constellation Uttar Phalguni</note> and the Fire-Deity <note>original: "Agneye"; refers to Krittika</note> are afflicted. Those in the Avanti region and from the Independence star <note>original: "Swati"; Arcturus</note> onwards are led into distress, as are those born in the Indus region <note>original: "Sindhu-desha"</note> by the Elder star <note>original: "Jyeshtha"; Antares</note>. 

In the regions of the traders <note>original: "Vaishya"</note> and the Harahas, the King of the Madra people is afflicted by the constellations of the Water-Lord <note>original: "Varuna"; refers to Shatabhisha</note> and the Wealthy One <note>original: "Revati"</note>. The people of Kuninda are tormented when the divisions of the mouth and tail are struck. Joined with white comets <note>original: "Shveta-abhis-charai"</note> and flanked on both sides, as shown in the embrace <note>original: "Ashlesha"; the constellation of the Serpent</note>, the Wheel of Constellations, O Great Goddess <note>an address to Parvati</note>, bestows both good and evil results. The heart represents the Middle Country, while the intermediate directions represent the theaters of war.

<margin>
Directed
</margin>

The face of the tortoise is the Eastern region <note>original: "Pragdesha"</note>, and the tail is the West, my dear one. The North and South directions are declared to be its two flanks. From the union of auspicious planets <note>original: "Shubha-graha," such as Jupiter or Venus</note>, there is good fortune in those directions. However, destruction occurs where malefic planets <note>original: "Papa-graha," such as Saturn or Mars</note> occupy the regions and their corresponding constellations.

In such cases, there is fear of injury by weapons, fever, and death, as well as the dread of disease and sickness when joined with those planets. When the Guru <note>original: "Jiva"; Jupiter</note>, Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note>, the Red-Bodied One <note>original: "Lohitanga"; Mars</note>, and Saturn <note>original: "Shanaishchara"</note> <gloss>are joined together</gloss> with the Son of the Sun <note>referring again to Saturn</note> in a single zodiac sign, some people are indeed struck down, some are destroyed by water, and some royal families are ruined or struck by foreign armies <note>original: "Para-chakra"</note>. In those times, flows of blood are seen on the earth.

### The Ritual of the Circle of Service

->**The Service Circle**<-

O Great Goddess <note>original: "Mahadevi"; a title for Parvati, the consort of Shiva</note>, I shall now clearly explain the **Service Circle** (*Seva-chakra*). By merely knowing this, a man can successfully pursue his goals in relation to his master <note>original: "svami"; can refer to a king, a deity, or an employer</note>.

Having first observed the ritual diagram <note>original: "nyasa"; the practice of "placing" mantras or energies on specific parts of the body or a diagram</note> on the ground, one should perform the placement. O fair-faced one <note>original: "Varanane"</note>, one should place seven [units] on the head, then eight, and seven on the heart, and exactly seven on the two feet, O Beloved <note>original: "Priye"</note>.

When the placement is on the **head**, one becomes most dear to the master, just as in ancient times. If the energy is situated there, one will undoubtedly receive honor and worship from the lord. 

O lady of good vows <note>original: "Suvrate"</note>, if placed [on the back], one obtains a tenth of the desired profit. If the placement is centered on the **heart**, the practitioner becomes a total servant <note>meaning: gains complete favor or becomes a devoted follower</note> of that master. Regarding the **feet**, all the fruits and results of "coming and going" <note>likely referring to successful travel, petitions, or messenger work</note> are attained. This has been told to you.

### The Signs of Longevity and the Path of Death

O lovely-faced one <note>a traditional address to the Goddess Parvati</note>, I shall specifically explain the Moon <note>original: "vidhu"</note>, the movement of the months, the lunar days <note>original: "tithi"</note>, and the auspicious periods <note>original: "pushkara"</note> regarding the end of days for the living, so that no doubt remains in the mind. 

If the breath stays fixed in the left or right channels <note>the Ida or Pingala nadis</note> while the Sun <note>original: "ravi"</note> is positioned in the tenth lunar mansion <note>original: "nakshatra"</note>, then that time is declared by the wise as "hostile" <note>original: "sapatna"; indicating an inauspicious or dangerous period</note>. Taking that moment as the starting point, the completion of the astrological union <note>original: "yoga"</note> occurs; from that point forward, the wise should begin their deliberation.

When the Lord God <note>referring to the Sun</note> transits into either the left or right side, the "mass of life" <note>original: "ayush-pinda"; the total allotted lifespan of an individual</note> is divided. A division is imagined there: the breath moves into the middle part <note>the Sushumna or central channel</note>. The Lord of the Day <note>the Sun</note> sets in the southern <note>right-side</note> path, and at night it returns to the middle again. 

Thus, practicing focused concentration <note>original: "samadhi"</note>, one should contemplate this in the mind. The signs of the lifespan can be seen clearly even when death is near. 

**The Sign of Three Years:**
If the breath <note>original: "vayu"</note> flows through only one path <note>constantly through only one nostril without alternating</note> for three full nights, O Parvati, then after three years have passed, that man will surely go to the abode of Yama <note>the God of Death</note>. 

For ten divine days, O divine sage, he [lives]... <note>the text breaks off here</note>

It is proclaimed that these cycles are nine-fold and are repeated three times, O beautiful one! Then, having arranged them within a grid of nine squares <term>kostha</term> <note>original: "kostha"; a cell or square in a diagram or table used for astrological calculations</note>, O lovely-faced one, one should place the Sun and the other planets in their proper order. 

Beginning with the nine-fold number, one should indicate the results of the planetary periods <term>dasha</term> <note>original: "dasha"; a specific period of time in an individual's life where a particular planet's influence is dominant</note> in their proper sequence for the purpose of astrological contemplation. 

For those born in the first category, the maximum lifespan <term>paramayu</term> <note>original: "paramayu"; the theoretical maximum age a person can reach based on their birth chart</note> is proclaimed to be one hundred years. In the second category, the lifespan is declared to be eighty years. In the third, it is sixty years. 

In the fourth category, one should calculate the maximum lifespan by measuring the specific time-units <term>nadikas</term> <note>original: "nadika"; a traditional Indian unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note>. One should divide by the portion of the lunar mansion <term>nakshatra</term> <note>original: "nakshatra"; one of the 27 sectors of the ecliptic through which the moon passes, used to determine birth characteristics</note> and subtract it at the end of the duration. By this method, the remaining portion of the lifespan for the living being becomes clear and precise <term>sphuti</term> <note>original: "sphuti"; a technical term in Indian astronomy meaning 'corrected' or 'calculated to the exact degree'</note>. 

From that total lifespan, the specific period belonging to each planet is then to be determined. Having derived the calculation for that period, one should divide it by the maximum lifespan. The result obtained should be multiplied by its own specific factor; that result constitutes the planetary period of the Sun and the others.

O Parvati, where there are twenty-seven lunar mansions <term>nakshatra</term> <note>original: "bhāni"; refers to the 27 stars or constellations of the Indian zodiac</note> in number, the calculation proceeds by starting from the birth star <term>janmarkṣa</term> <note>original: "janmarkṣa"; the constellation the moon was in at the moment of birth</note>. If the calculation is for general purposes, it should be done from the star associated with one's name, O fair-complexioned one!

In the subsequent contemplation, whether regarding the reason for leaving one’s country or when a malefic <term>pāpa</term> <note>original: "pāpa"; literally 'sinful' or 'wicked', referring to planets like Saturn or Mars that traditionally bring challenges</note> planetary period is reached: if the planet is strong, it is remembered as a bringer of death. If it is weak, it causes the loss of wealth, O fair-complexioned one!

<summary>This page details the mathematical breakdown of planetary periods into days and months and explains that calculations should begin from the birth star or name star. It warns that a strong malefic planet during its period can signify death, while a weak one signifies financial loss.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the discourse on astrological timing. Endowed with medium strength, a planet is remembered as causing affliction. However, through the worship of the gods, through acts of charity, by the company of holy people, and through the blessings of the learned <term>vipra</term> <note>original: "viprāṇām"; referring to the priestly or Brahmin class</note>, untimely death <term>apamṛtyu</term> <note>a death occurring before the destined lifespan is completed, often thought to be preventable through ritual</note> is destroyed.

The planets are known to be the lords of the lunar mansions <term>nakshatras</term>. Then, O dear one, in sequence, follow the others. During the twelve sub-periods <term>antar-daśā</term> <note>minor cycles nested within the major planetary periods</note>, when the Sun <term>Sūrya</term> is the ruler, there will be loss from the royal family, suffering, and misfortune from the father's side, as well as various expenditures of wealth for men.

<margin>Chaitra 6 <note>A date in the Hindu lunar calendar, likely marking when the scribe reached this point in the transcription</note></margin>

When the Moon <term>Candra</term> is the ruler: the gain of women, sons, and land; the enjoyment and accumulation of ornaments; enmity with one's own kin; union with a maiden; sleep, and sexual pleasure occur.

When Mars <term>Bhauma</term> is the ruler: the crushing of enemies and conflict with relatives. **Suffering caused by blood and bile <note>original: "rakta-pitta"; a medical condition in Indian medicine (Ayurveda) involving internal heat and bleeding</note> and association with the wives of others.**

**[The sub-period results continue]** When Rahu <term>Svarbhānu</term> <note>original: "Svarbhānu"; another name for the shadow planet Rahu, the North Node of the Moon</note> is active: the death of a wife and conflict will occur. There is loss of wealth and arrival in a foreign land where one receives little respect.

When the planet of knowledge <note>likely referring to Jupiter, the "Guru"</note> is known to be active: an increase in sons, the arrival of wealth and righteousness <term>dharma</term>. The acquisition of gold, horses, and clothing is certain.

When Saturn <term>Śanaiścara</term> is active: bodily suffering and conflict with one’s sons and wife. <sig>dha</sig> <note>Catchword for the next page beginning with "dha"</note>

[Continued from the previous page...] ...much happiness and the gain of wealth, but otherwise everything is inauspicious.

During the **major period of Rahu** <note>the ascending lunar node</note>, when the sub-periods of Jupiter <term>Jīva</term>, Mercury <term>Jña</term>, and Venus <term>Śukra</term> arrive, they grant gain. However, the sub-periods of Ketu, the Moon <term>Indu</term>, the Sun <term>Ravi</term>, and Saturn <term>Śani</term> are remembered to bring great sorrow.

If, within [the major period of the Moon], the sub-periods of the Moon <term>Indu</term>, the Sun <term>Ravi</term>, Mercury <term>Jña</term>, Mars <term>Āra</term>, and Jupiter <term>Jīva</term> occur, they result in all manner of gain and great happiness; but the malefic planets <note>planets with a naturally cruel influence</note> act as destroyers.

If, within the sub-period of Saturn <term>Saura</term>, there appear Jupiter <term>Guru</term>, Venus <term>Śukra</term>, or Mercury <term>Vit</term> <note>original: "viṭasya"; likely a variant for Budha/Mercury</note>,

they grant gain, happiness, and respect; death is ordained for the remaining planetary sub-periods.

Within the sub-period of Mercury <term>Budha</term>, the influence of Venus <term>Śukra</term>, Jupiter <term>Jīva</term>, the Sun <term>Ravi</term>, and Saturn <term>Saura</term> leads to victory. 

Within the major period of Mars <term>Bhauma</term>, the sub-periods of the Moon <term>Candra</term>, Ketu, and the Sun <term>Ravi</term> cause the loss of wealth. 

Within the sub-period of Ketu, only when Jupiter <term>Jīva</term> is present does true good fortune <term>saubhāgya</term> occur. Within the sub-periods of the Moon and Mars, it is also auspicious, while the remaining planets are the bringers of suffering.

Jupiter <term>Jīva</term>, Saturn <term>Saura</term>, and Mercury <term>Budha</term> are the planets <term>khagā</term> <note>literally "sky-travelers"</note> that, when moving within the major period of Venus <term>Śukra</term>, produce all gains and victory; the others are indeed condemned as inauspicious.

Thus is remembered the fruit of the ripening <term>pāka</term> <note>the "cooking" or maturation of karmic results</note> of time, based on the strength of planetary friends and enemies. 

Regarding the Sun <term>Sūrya</term>, those born on the days of Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury are the bringers of auspiciousness. The number for the Sun <note>original: "dinakara", the day-maker</note> is twenty <note>original: "nakha"; literally "fingernails," a traditional Sanskrit code for the number 20</note>. The Moon's number is according to the days of the week... <note>the text cuts off mid-calculation</note>

According to the knowledge of the Grandfather <note>original: "Pitāmaha-jñaḥ"; referring to the Brahma-Siddhanta school of astronomy</note>, the six solar influencers and Saturn are spoken of with the qualities of the essence. <meta>This page continues the technical discourse on astrological charts. While the previous page introduced the "Deer Chart," this page details the "Sun Cycle" (Ravi Chakra) and provides general principles for interpreting planetary positions—such as exaltation, debilitation, and aspects—to determine a person's destiny, wealth, and character.</meta>

<margin>
Pustaka <note>Book/Page</note>
Birth Record No.
42
</margin>

The auspicious and inauspicious Sun Cycle <note>original: "ravi-cakram"</note> is to be understood by practitioners for the purpose of interpreting a birth horoscope <term>Jataka</term>. These influences manifest clearly in the angular houses <term>Kendra</term> <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a horoscope</note> and the trine houses <term>Trikona</term> <note>the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note>. A planet is considered auspicious when it resides in the house of a friendly planet or is aspected <note>viewed through a planetary "glance"</note> by a friend. Even malefic planets <note>original: "pāpepi"</note>, if they are in their sign of exaltation <term>Uccha</term> or situated in the houses of growth <term>Upachaya</term> <note>the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</note>, are said to bestow royal power at birth. When planets are in their own exaltation or a friend’s house, located in an angle, and combined with the aspect of a friend—or if benefics are in the houses of growth—they all become planets that bestow total fortune. Conversely, all planets situated in their sign of debilitation <term>Nicha</term> or in an enemy's house, combined with cruel aspects, or those scorched by the Sun’s rays <note>original: "arka-rasmi-vyāgrutāḥ"; referring to "combustion," where a planet's influence is weakened by being too close to the Sun</note>, result in sorrow and a lack of wealth. If the Lord of the Ascendant <term>Lagna-pati</term> is situated in an angle or in its own house, it bestows all manner of gains. When planets aspect the Ascendant with auspicious influences, and if a strong benefic planet is in its exaltation, its own divisional chart <term>Varga</term>, or its own house, that person will always share in royal power, being a generous giver endowed with many virtues.

## **Thus, in the glorious *Tantra of the Ocean of Victory in Battle*, in the dialogue between**
## **the Goddess Uma and the Lord Maheshvara <note>Shiva and Parvati</note>, follows the deliberation on birth horoscopes**
## **according to the *Svarodaya* <note>the science of breath and sound divination</note>, for the purpose of attaining the auspicious and inauspicious fruits of the Time Wheel <term>Kalachakra</term>. This is the**
## **sixth chapter <term>Patala</term>. 6. Total verses: 537. Now, therefore, I shall fully declare**

the Path Chart <term>Pathachakra</term>, a great wonder, which constantly provides the results for journeys <term>Yatra</term> and acts as an immediate, direct proof. Even without the specific calculations of the stars beginning with Ashvini, [the results] are adorned by the pulses <term>Nadis</term> <note>referring to astrological channels or energy currents</note> through which they pass. Listen, O beloved one, to the sequence of stars beginning with Ashvini:

The first channel <term>Nadi</term> is located in the South <note>original: "yāmye"</note>. It consists of Ashvini, Punarvasu <note>original: "aditi"</note>, Uttaraphalguni, Hasta, Jyeshtha, Mula, Shatabhisha <note>original: "vāruṇaṃ"</note>, and Purvabhadrapada <note>original: "ajaikabhaṃ"</note>.

The second channel is proclaimed, O Goddess of Deities, to consist of Mrigashirsha, Pushya <note>original: "tiṣya"</note>, Purvaphalguni <note>original: "bhāgya"</note>, Chitra, Anuradha <note>original: "maitraṃ"</note>, Dhanishtha <note>original: "vāsavamaṃ"</note>, and Uttarabhadrapada <note>original: "ahirbudhnyaṃ"</note>.

The third and final channel consists of Krittika, Rohini, Ashlesha <note>original: "sarpe"</note>, Magha <note>original: "pitryaṃ"</note>, Swati, Vishakha <note>original: "dvidaitava"</note>, Uttarashadha <note>original: "viśvedevā"</note>, Shravana <note>original: "vaiṣṇavaṃ"</note>, and Revati <note>original: "pauṣṇaṃ"</note>.

O beloved, one must determine the auspicious and inauspicious through the conjunction of planets within these stars. For a traveler, the influence is determined by the first mansion of the channel where the planets are situated...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the "Path Chart" (Pathachakra), transitioning from general planetary influences to specific calculations involving retrograde motion and the introduction of a specialized "Death Chart" (Mrityu-chakra). One should indicate the acquisition of an uncountable number of connections. In the "faces" of the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras</term> <note>the 27 or 28 sectors of the sky along the moon's path</note>, the explanation of retrograde motion <term>Vakra</term> <note>the apparent backward movement of a planet in the sky</note> is made in conjunction with malefic signs. By parts of the beginning of the signs, with brilliant qualities, and by planets in triple retrograde motion, one should specify the calculation. 

The Sun <note>original: "Arkā"</note> should also be multiplied here, as practiced by the perfected masters <term>Siddhas</term>. The knower of the conjunction of planets and stars, and of the retrograde motion within the planetary circles, whether in front, behind, or alongside, or moving through the degrees—this chart, **O Great Goddess** <note>original: "Mahādevi"</note>, is a producer of true conviction.

Alternatively, I shall describe it in another way; listen, **O Mother** <note>original: "Ambike"</note>. 

The chart is drawn starting with the lunar mansion **Ardra** <note>the 'Moist One,' a star associated with the god Rudra</note> and is adorned with the Three Channels <term>Trinadi</term>. In the flow of the channels of the Sun and the Moon, that specific channel brings death <note>original: "Mṛtyudā"</note>. When a channel is equal to a malefic planet <term>Papagraha</term>, the number is destroyed like a deer. 

Listen, **Gauri** <note>another name for the Goddess, meaning 'The Golden One'</note>, to the supreme secret of the **Death Chart** <term>Mrityu-chakra</term> fashioned by me. The chart is written starting with Ardra and is surrounded by the Three Channels. Through the state of the Sun and Moon, the three channels arise. In that state, fear and death are certain, O Supreme Goddess <note>original: "Parameśvarī"</note>. Therefore...

I shall now fully describe the **Shield Chart** <term>Khetaka Chakra</term> <note>original: "Khetakaṃ"; "Khetaka" typically means a shield, suggesting this chart acts as a protection or a "covering" for the person inquiring</note>. Distinguishing the day of the week <term>Grahawara</term> and combining it with the lunar date <term>Tithi</term>, one should subtract one <note>original: "rūpa hīne"; literally "diminished by one (form)"</note>. Then, one should divide the result by seven and two <note>original: "sapta-netraiśca"; "netra" (eyes) is a traditional code for the number 2</note>. 

Regarding the remainder: in that triad, one finds the acquisition of results through the paths of the Sun and other planets. In certain parts, one might flee. The wise <note>original: "budhaiḥ"</note> should provide an explanation based on the zodiac sign <term>Rashi</term> and the lunar mansion <term>Nakshatra</term>. 

In the Shield Chart, the day may be auspicious, and that should be declared; however, it can also bring about misfortune, disease, and sorrow. It may signify death <note>original: "naidhanam"</note>, the crushing of limbs, withdrawal, destruction in the city, or stumbling at the time of birth. If the remainder is good, the person who knows the calculation <term>Gannavide</term> should declare a beautiful result. 

This Shield Chart is also called the **Illusion Chart** <term>Maya Chakra</term> in the tradition. This is the **Shield Chart of Death**.

A king should examine the **Outcome Chart** <term>Aya Chakra</term> <note>original: "Āya-cakraṃ"; 'Aya' refers to 'gain' or 'income,' but in this divinatory context, it refers to a specific calculation used to determine the strength and lifespan of a person or a project</note> during a time of war. In that process, one should draw ten vertical lines and six horizontal lines. <margin>Space 1</margin> [One should designate] the "Manu" (14) and "Netra" (2) <gloss>24</gloss> <gloss>8</gloss> <gloss>23</gloss> <gloss>22</gloss> <note>The Sanskrit uses "Manu" for 14 and "Netra" for 2; the glosses provide the specific numerical results used in the calculation</note>.

By dividing the sum by eight <term>Vasubhir</term> <note>original: "Vasu" refers to the 8 Vasu deities, used as a code for the number 8</note>, the remainder determines the categories such as Banner <term>Dhvaja</term>, Deer <term>Mriga</term>, and the others.

These are known as the eight categories: Banner <term>Dhvaja</term>, Smoke <term>Dhūma</term> <note>implied</note>, Lion <term>Siṃha</term>, Dog <term>Śva</term>, Bull <term>Vṛṣa</term>, Donkey <term>Khara</term>, Elephant <term>Gaja</term>, and Crow <term>Kāka</term>. One should contemplate their nature in this way: 

In this system, the Dog is stronger than the Crow; the Donkey is stronger than the Dog; the Bull is stronger than the Donkey; the Elephant is stronger than the Bull; and the **Lion** <term>Panchasya</term> <note>original: "Pañcāsya"; literally "five-faced," a common poetic name for the lion</note> is stronger even than the Elephant.

Now, another logical method for the extraction of the letters of a name <term>Namakshara</term> is demonstrated. In the case of odd and even fields, having obtained the results separately for both, victory is certain in an odd field if the number is greater. In an even field, the result is as previously stated.
When the remainder is divided by four, if three remain, victory is assured. In the case of the fourth being greater than the other, in an odd sequence of the two, whoever holds the higher number shall be the victor. If the two numbers are equal, then a decrease or increase of one is considered.

As for the measurement of the wound, it is determined there by "Vamshakas" <note>literal: "bamboo-lengths"; here a proportional unit of measurement</note> and **finger-widths** <term>Angulas</term> <note>a traditional unit of measurement approximately equal to the width of a finger</note>. At the end of the strike, the count is one finger-width. Due to the reduction or the strength of the portions, those aspects seen by the **Sun** <term>Ravi</term> <note>In astrology, "seen by" refers to a planetary aspect</note> indicate various types of life-force; those seen by **Rahu** <note>The North Node of the Moon, associated with eclipse and chaos</note> lead to destruction; and those seen by **Saturn** <term>Shanishchara</term> lead to the fall of the master or leader.

### The Battle Outcome via the Trident Diagram
### <term>Trishula Chakra</term>

Now, the timing and outcome of a battle <term>sangrāma</term> are determined through the **Trident Diagram** <term>Trishula Chakra</term>. This includes whether there will be victory in battle, no battle at all, a minor skirmish, or great suffering. ॥

In this Trident, which has three parts, one begins by marking the head. The three lunar mansions <term>nakshatras</term> currently "embraced by the Sun" <note>original: āditya lingitā; refers to the mansions occupied or influenced by the Sun's current position</note> are placed upon the three points or prongs <term>koṭi</term> of the trident. 

Following this, the remaining lunar mansions are placed on the handle <term>daṇḍa</term> of the trident, with six mansions calculated for the exterior. Then, place three at the entering tips of the trident, three within the interior, and three more at the tip of the handle. 

In this arrangement, three mansions are placed in each section <note>original: trika; a group of three</note>. One calculates the order of these mansions to determine the "expenditure" or loss <term>vyaya</term> for whoever is involved in the conflict. By counting the three at the points of the battle-chart in their proper sequence, the middle of the trident is established.

Based on whether the mansions are situated on the right or the left side of the trident, one determines the "entry" or "exit" from the field of combat. According to the teachings of **Sound-Breath Divination** <term>Swarodaya</term> <note>A system of divination based on the flow of breath through the nostrils and its relationship to planetary cycles</note>, one can predict if there will be a retreat <term>palāyana</term> or an injury <term>ghāta</term> within two days. Subsequently, the actual clash of the battle occurs, and the fighting continues for two periods of time.

<vocab>Trishula Chakra (Trident Diagram), Sangrama (Battle), Swarodaya (Science of Breath/Phonetic Divination), Yuddha (War/Combat), Trishula (Trident)</vocab>

In that **Triangular Diagram** <term>Tribhuja Yantra</term> <note>original: Tribhuja-yantra; here refers to the three-pronged trident diagram used for military or tactical divination</note>, the specific position within the battle is determined. Then, the **Great Battle Trident** <term>Mahahavashula</term> <note>original: Mahāhavashūla; literally "Great Combat Spear/Trident," a specific divinatory layout</note> is established upon the three central points <term>koti</term>.

If these points are "withdrawn" or blocked, then combat <term>āji</term> will surely follow. After that, death may occur; such are the verses <term>mantras</term> governing this science. By the influence of malefic planets <note>original: pāpaiḥ; refers to planets like Mars or Saturn</note>, the outcome shall be inauspicious, but through benefic planets <note>original: shobhanaiḥ; refers to planets like Jupiter or Venus</note>, the outcome shall be auspicious.

Having drawn the three prongs of the trident, and meditating upon the seed-syllable **Ram** <note>original: raṃ; the sacred sound associated with the element of fire, invoked here to empower the martial diagram</note>, one must proceed in sequence. Three by three, nine lunar mansions <term>nakshatras</term> are placed at the tips of the trident. In the same manner, the remaining eighteen lunar mansions are to be placed in the intervals between the prongs.

I shall now proclaim the results of the mansions located on the trident prongs in their proper order. From these positions come affliction <term>pīḍā</term> and the fear of disease <term>rogabhīti</term>.

### **The Great Lord Spoke:**

Even after contemplating the Diagram of the Stars <term>Riksha Chakra</term>, an undertaking should not begin [without this ritual placement]. One should arrange fourteen squares horizontally and place the [stars/animals] within them.

### The Classification of Stable and Unstable Influences

The lunar mansions <term>Nakshatra: the 27 or 28 divisions of the sky through which the Moon passes</term> known as Ardrā <note>original: Raundrī</note>, Pūrvāṣāḍhā, Maghā, and Shatabhisha <note>original: Vāruṇaṃ</note> are called **Kulākula** <note>literally "Stable-Unstable"; a mixed influence</note>. On these days, harm may come to one's own family or lineage, yet there is victory when rising in the East. In other mansions, there is the loss of men. Victory is determined by the lunar day <term>Tithi: a lunar day in the Vedic calendar</term>; specifically, victory is achieved on **Fridays** <note>original: Śukravāra</note>. In a great battle, victory comes to those who divide into pairs.

The second, fifth, and sixth lunar days are declared to be **Kulākula**. All odd-numbered lunar days are **Akula** <note>literally "without family"; unstable or erratic</note>, and the remaining lunar days are **Kula** <note>literally "of the family"; stable and auspicious</note>. 2

The Moon, Mars, and Saturn—these four <note>referring to planets/days</note> are also **Kula**. 3
Friday is called **Akula**, while Tuesday <note>original: Bhaumavāra</note> is **Kulākula**.

### The Sight of the Stars <term>Nakshatra-Netra</term>

Ashvinī, the auspicious lunar days, Punarvasu <note>original: Āditi</note>, and Shravana—these seven are "endowed with eyes" <note>original: netra-yuktāḥ</note> and bestow auspiciousness. They reveal everything that is lost or hidden.

Ardrā, Mūla, Shatabhisha <note>original: Vāruṇaṃ</note>, Svāti, and the three "Prior" Mansions <note>Pūrvā Phālguni, Pūrvāṣāḍhā, and Pūrvā Bhādrapadā</note> are "devoid of eyes" <note>original: netrā-hīnā; often interpreted as "blind"</note>. They grant success in all types of work.

Maghā, Vishākhā, Jyeshṭhā, Rohiṇī, and the three "Latter" Mansions <note>Uttarā Phālguni, Uttarāṣāḍhā, and Uttarā Bhādrapadā</note> are described as "blind in the feet" <note>original: kāyo-caraṇāḥ</note>. For some specific tasks... <unclear>shra</unclear>

<summary>This page details the Kulākula system for predicting success in battle and the Netra (Eyes) classification of Nakshatras used to find lost objects or ensure the success of a venture.</summary>
<keywords>Kulākula, Akula, Kula, Nakshatra, Tithi, Netra-yukta, Netra-hina, victory, astrology, planetary days</keywords>

These charts classify the stars, planets, and lunar days into four "lineages" or "families" (Kulas). This categorization is a technical diagnostic tool in Hindu astrology used to determine the compatibility and results of actions—specifically whether an event belongs to one's own sphere of influence or an external one.</meta>

<note>This table categorizes the 27 lunar mansions (plus the intercalary Abhijit) into three rows. The first row represents the Divine family (Deva Kula), the second the Human family (Manusha Kula), and the third represents a "Mixed" or "Stable-Unstable" (Kulākula) influence. These classifications help an astrologer decide if a day is naturally harmonious or potentially volatile.</note>

### The Auspicious Stars and the Four Sights

The illuminators <note>the stars that bring clarity to a situation</note> are **Bharani**, **Krittika**, **Uttara Phalguni**, **Ardra**, **Dhanistha**, **Abhijit**, and **Revati**. <note>original: Bharani-Krittika-Saryama-Ardra-Vasubha-Abhijit-Paushna. 'Saryama' refers to the deity of Uttara Phalguni, and 'Vasubha' refers to Dhanistha.</note> When these are combined in the *Chipata* <note>a technical term in divination for a specific planetary position or mathematical remainder</note>, they are bestowers of auspiciousness. They bring profit in the use of wealth and money. On those days, one should avoid <unclear>loss</unclear>.

Now follow the categories of "Eyesight": **Blind-eyed**, followed by **Dim-eyed**, **Middle-eyed**, and **Good-eyed**. <term>Nakshatra-Lochana: a system where the 27 lunar mansions are divided into four groups to predict the outcome of a theft.</term>

Starting from **Rohini**, the four groups of stars are assigned to the East, South, West, and North. If a theft occurs when the star is "Blind-eyed," the gain <note>recovery of the item</note> is certain and the object is nearby. If it is "Dim-eyed," there is a slow recovery. If it is "Middle-eyed," it is at a distance. If it is "Good-eyed," the object is far away. In the *Chipata* calculation, if it falls in the "Good-eyed" category, recovery is not possible.

<meta>This page continues the astrological technicalities from page 73. While the previous page focused on the "eyesight" of the stars to locate stolen property, this page shifts to a broader functional classification of the Nakshatras (lunar mansions). It categorizes the twenty-eight stars into seven temperament groups—Fixed, Gentle, Swift, Movable, Sharp, Mixed, and Fierce—to determine their suitability for various activities like war, business, or travel.</meta>

[Page 74]

<note>There is faint ghosting/bleed-through of text from the reverse page visible on the right-hand side of the leaf.</note>

The **Fixed**, **Gentle**, **Swift**, **Movable**, **Sharp**, **Mixed**, and **Fierce**—the lunar mansions <note>original: Nakshatras</note> are divided into these seven distinct groups. Their results in life are consistent with these names.

Regarding their effects: **Ashvini**, **Hasta**, and **Pushya** <note>original: Ashvini-Hasta-Pushya-iti</note> are known as the **Swift** <note>original: Laghu</note> group, which consists of four stars <note>including Abhijit, as seen in the table below</note>. **Rohini** and the three **Uttaras** <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note> are known as the **Fixed** <note>original: Dhruva</note> stars.

The **Gentle** <note>original: Mridu</note> group consists of **Mrigashirsha**, **Chitra**, **Revati** <note>original: Paushna</note>, and **Anuradha** <note>original: Maitra</note>. The **Sharp** <note>original: Daruna</note> group includes **Ashlesha** <note>original: Sarpa</note>, **Ardra**, **Mula**, and **Jyeshtha**.

O beautiful one, the three stars beginning with **Shravana** <note>Shravana, Dhanistha, and Shatabhisha</note>, along with **Swati** and **Punarvasu** <note>original: Aditya</note>, are designated as **Movable** <note>original: Chara</note>. O dear one, the three **Purvas** <note>Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, along with **Magha** <note>original: Pitru</note> and **Bharani**, are known as the **Fierce** <note>original: Ugra</note> group.

Finally, **Krittika** and **Vishakha** are called the **Mixed** <note>original: Mishra</note> stars. These very powerful classifications are used when going into battle, engaging in commerce, or performing any personal task, as the results of one’s actions will follow the nature of the star—be it movable, sharp, or ordinary.

<note>This verse assigns animal archetypes to the phonetic groups of the Sanskrit alphabet (Ka-varga, Cha-varga, etc.).</note>

<meta>This page continues the technical exposition of the *Vargachakra* (Alphabetical Circle), moving from the general classification of animal and spirit archetypes to a specific mathematical and directional grid. It establishes a complex correspondence between the Sanskrit phonetic groups (Vargas), the planets (Grahas), the lunar days (Tithis), and the cardinal directions to facilitate advanced astrological divination.</meta>

In the groups, facing forward: the **vowel-group** <note>original: avarga; the group of letters beginning with 'A'</note> belongs to the **Sun** <note>original: arka</note> and the **Moon** <note>original: chandra</note>. In the second group are **Mars** <note>original: bhauma</note>, **Mercury** <note>original: budha</note>, **Jupiter** <note>original: guru</note>, **Venus** <note>original: shukra</note>, and **Saturn** <note>original: shani</note>.

**Rahu** <note>the North Node</note> and **Ketu** <note>the South Node</note> are associated with the **first** <note>Pratipada</note> and **ninth** lunar days. In the **'ta' group** <note>the dental consonants: ta, tha, da, dha, na</note>, O Goddess, are the **second** and **tenth** lunar days. In the **'ka' group** <note>the velar consonants: ka, kha, ga, gha, nga</note> is the **Southeast** direction <note>original: agneyi</note> and the **fourth** and **twelfth** lunar days. In the **'ta' group** <note>the retroflex consonants: ṭa, ṭha, ḍa, ḍha, ṇa</note> is the **Southwest** direction <note>original: nairiti</note>.

| Venus / Moon 8 <br> **Human** 15 <br> sound: Am | vowel: A <br> **God** 1 <br> 8 <br> sound: Am | **Demon** 2 <br> Ketu / Ka-group 9 |
| :--- | :---: | ---: |
| Ya-group 7 <br> Jupiter 10 <br> Vishakha <note>a star constellation</note> 17 | **Eight-fold Class Circle of the Serpent** <br> **Tiva Class Category** | sound: Chai 3 <br> Va-group 11 |
| Ya-group 6 <br> Mars 14 <br> **Ogre** 14 | **Sage** 5 <br> Na-group 13 <br> Tuli <note>likely Libra/Tula</note> | **Celestial Musician** 4 <br> Ha / Ra sounds 12 |

<margin>ta 3</margin>
The **fifth** and **thirteenth** lunar days belong to the **'cha' group** <note>the palatal consonants: ca, cha, ja, jha, nya</note> and the **West** direction.
The **sixth** and **fourteenth** belong to the **'pa' group** <note>the labial consonants: pa, pha, ba, bha, ma</note> and the **Northwest** direction.
The **seventh** and the **Full Moon** <note>original: purnima</note> belong to the **'ya' group** <note>the semi-vowels: ya, ra, la, va</note> and the **North** direction.

<summary>This page details the 'Ashtagana Chakra' (Eight-fold Class Circle), a divinatory grid that maps Sanskrit phonetic groups to specific lunar days, directions, and planetary influences. It functions as a specialized tool for calculating the compatibility of names or determining auspicious timings based on the intersection of sound and celestial movement.</summary>

The eighth lunar day <note>original: Ashtami</note> and the New Moon <note>original: Amavasya</note>, in their own phonetic group, should be placed in the **Northeast direction** <note>original: Ishana-dig</note>. Calculations regarding **medicines**, **sacred chants** <note>original: mantra</note>, and **battles** <note>original: sangrama</note> should be performed; these are not for the deceased but for **living humans**. Success <note>original: siddhi</note> is achieved when these align within one's own phonetic group or the group associated with one's astrological house, and not otherwise.

The **Gods** <term>original: Devas; the celestial beings</term> are said to be represented by the **vowel-group** <term>original: Akara-varga; the group of letters starting with 'A'</term>. The **Demons** <term>original: Daityas</term> are known to be born from the **ka-group** <term>original: Ka-varga; the velar consonants: ka, kha, ga, gha, nga</term>. The **Serpents** <term>original: Nagas</term> and **Celestial Musicians** <term>original: Gandharvas</term> are remembered as belonging to the **cha-group** <term>original: Cha-varga; the palatal consonants: cha, chha, ja, jha, nya</term>. The **Sages** <term>original: Munis</term> come from the **ta-group** <term>original: Ta-varga; the retroflex consonants: ṭa, ṭha, ḍa, ḍha, ṇa</term>. The **Ogres** <term>original: Rakshasas</term> belong to the **pa-group** <term>original: Pa-varga; the labial consonants: pa, pha, ba, bha, ma</term>.

<meta>This page continues the technical breakdown of avian divination (Shakuna Shastra), specifically mapping the fluctuating power of five primary birds (the Vulture, Hawk, Owl, Spotted Owlet, and Crane) across the lunar calendar and seasons. It builds upon the previous page's introduction of these five animals as symbolic representations of planetary influences.</meta>

->१५४<-

At twilight, the **Owl** <term>original: Kaushika</term> is strong during the **arrival of the blossoms** <note>original: kusumagame; referring to the Spring season</note>. The female worship-birds are also powerful in the **month of Nabhasya** <note>the lunar month of Bhadrapada, roughly August–September</note>. 

The **Gangovatsaka** <note>likely a type of heron or water-bird</note> and the **Kauchaka** are unassailable and remain equal in strength at all times. 

The relative strength of birds based on the lunar day <term>original: tithi</term> is as follows:

*   The **Vulture** <term>original: Gridhra</term> is designated as powerful on the **first** <note>Pratipada</note> and **ninth** <note>Navami</note> days of the lunar fortnight.
*   On the **second** <note>Dvitiya</note> and **tenth** <note>Dashami</note> days, the **Hawk** <term>original: Shyena</term> is the dominant bird.
*   On the **third** <note>Tritiya</note> and **eleventh** <note>Ekadashi</note> days, the **Owls** <term>original: Kaushika</term> are likewise strong.
*   The **Spotted Owlet** <term>original: Pingala</term> is powerful on the **fourth** <note>Chaturthi</note> and **twelfth** <note>Dvadashi</note> days.
*   The **Crane** <term>original: Sarasa</term> is superior and "unbearable" <note>meaning its power is overwhelming</note> on the **fifth** <note>Panchami</note> and **thirteenth** <note>Trayodashi</note> days.
*   The **Peacock** <term>original: Mayura</term>, even when great, is the ruler of the **sixth** <note>Shashthi</note> and **fourteenth** <note>Chaturdashi</note> days.
*   On the **seventh** <note>Saptami</note> and **fifteenth** <note>Panchadashi; the Full or New Moon</note> days, the **Bird of Omens** <term>original: Shakuni</term> is strong.
*   On the **eighth** <note>Ashtami</note> day alone, the **Govatsa** <note>literally "cow-calf" bird, likely a lapwing or similar ground bird</note> possesses heavy prowess.

In this very manner, the power of these **twice-born** <note>original: dvijati; a standard term for birds because they are born first as an egg and then as a hatchling</note> expands and manifests. From the Vulture to the others, their sequence follows the Sun and Fire. One must calculate their strength according to the divisions of the month, the day, the night, and the specific lunar date.

Similarly, other birds should be understood here through their great strength. One must thoroughly investigate the divisions of species and their **relative strengths and weaknesses** <term>original: balabala</term>. As for the **Spotted Owlet**...

### The Serpent Chart

Having drawn the **Serpent Chart** <term>original: Sarpa-Chakra</term>, one should write the constellations in order from the head down to the tail. One must carefully place the stars upon the mouth, the throat, the shoulders, the navel, the waist, and the tail. By writing them in groups of three, one should contemplate the following results:

*   **The Throat, Stomach, Waist, and Tail:** If these areas are joined with malefic influences <term>original: papa-samyuta</term>, a scholar should predict death or great peril.
*   **The Chest:** This area requires special attention regarding life force.
*   **The Mouth and Navel:** These positions indicate either the arrival of wealth or a state of renunciation <note>original: tyajyo; literally "to be abandoned," often referring to giving up worldly goods or a situation to be avoided</note>.
*   **The Shoulders:** On the shoulder, one should recognize the sign of victory.
*   **The Buttocks and Tail:** These indicate the finality of the matter.

Listen now to the movement of the constellations in the chart named **The Donkey** <note>original: Khara; in Sanskrit, this can refer to a specific year in the 60-year cycle or a sharp/harsh temperament in divination</note>. Writing them in the sequence beginning with the knots <note>original: granthi</note>, for the sake of the **Beloved of the Ganas** <note>original: Gana-priye; likely an invocation to Ganesha or a specific deity associated with these calculations</note>, the rows of steps should be placed in order...

### **Thus, in the glorious *Yuddhajayarnava Tantra*, in the dialogue between Uma and Maheshvara**
### **According to the *Svarodaya* [Science of Breath and Sound], the Method of Three-fold Star Alignment**
### **The Eighth Chapter, named "The Chart." The Lord spoke:**

Listen, O Goddess, to the ultimate secret named the **Throne** <note>original: Simhasana</note>. This chart, which is mentioned in other sacred scriptures, is extremely rare. It has not been revealed to anyone else, as it is a producer of direct conviction and proof. 

The groups of days and planetary cycles are inconceivable, starting with the Ancestors <note>original: pitradhyashcha; likely referring to the Pitris, the presiding deities of the constellation Magha</note>. Following that, the groupings starting from the "Root" <note>original: muladi; referring to the constellation Mula</note> are joined; one must consider which group belongs there. From these, nine are remembered according to the path of virtue <note>original: dharmatah</note>. 

These are to be understood throughout various lands. Beyond those, O beautiful one among the gods, are the constellations Dhanistha, Mrigashirsha, and thirdly, the one ruled by the deity Tvashtri <note>original: tvashtra-daivatam; this refers to the constellation Chitra</note>. O Ruler of the Goddesses, this is proclaimed in order as the **Throne Chart** <term>original: Simhasanaravyam; a grid used in martial or political divination to predict the "seating" or stability of a king or commander</term>.

Dhanistha is located in the northern region. The center is auspicious <note>original: saumyam; can also mean 'belonging to the Moon' or 'North'</note>, O Parvati, and it is the same in the eastern region. This includes Chitra, O Beloved... <unclear>pare</unclear> ...gods...

O beautiful one, one must contemplate in sequence the **Parasol** <term>original: Chatra; a traditional symbol of royal authority</term>, the **Whisks** <term>original: Chamara; fans made of yak-tail used in royal courts</term>, the **Throne** <note>original: Simhasana</note>, and likewise the **Golden Knot**. The ninth position is the place of **Virtue** <note>original: Dharmasthanam; the house of law or religion</note>.

One should observe the order where the **Son of the Sun** <note>original: Suryaputra; a name for the planet Saturn, traditionally associated with hardship and karma</note> is situated. If he is placed in the Parasol, on the Throne, or in the Whisks, O Ruler of the Goddesses, one should declare the results in this sequence: the death of the king, or his worship <note>referring to rituals of propitiation or perhaps his funeral rites</note>, and then rain, O Goddess.

In the Throne or the celestial chart, all depends on the service of the "Throw" <note>likely referring to the casting of the planetary positions</note>. By this very method, the chart is adorned by the four and three <note>perhaps referring to the seven layers or a specific 4x3 grid division</note>.

All the positions are connected to the **Channels** <note>original: nadis; subtle energy currents</note>, three constellations <note>original: rikshani</note> assigned to each. On the outer part: **Mrigashirsha** <note>The Deer-Head constellation</note>, **Chitra** <note>The Bright constellation</note>, and **Dhanishtha** <note>original: Nishtha; The Wealthy constellation</note> are proclaimed to be the **Staff of the Parasol**.

If these are joined with the **Sun** <note>original: Ravi</note>, **Rahu** <note>The shadow planet of eclipses</note>, and **Mars** <note>original: Kuja</note>, or associated with **Saturn** <note>original: Shani</note> and Rahu—if they fall first in the Deer constellation <note>Mrigashirsha</note> and are combined with the "Victorious" <note>original: jaji; possibly a specific astrological yoga or aspect</note>, there is no doubt of the outcome. Likewise, if combined with the constellation Chitra and malefic planets, or with **Mercury** <note>original: Budha</note>, the result is middling. When Saturn is pacified, the **King’s Chart** <term>original: Nripachakra; a specialized divination tool used to predict the fate of monarchs and their kingdoms</term> is thus inscribed.

->Viteh<- <note>likely a concluding marker or specific technical designation</note>

<vocab>Chatra (Parasol), Chamara (Whisk), Simhasana (Throne), Ravi (Sun), Rahu (Eclipse shadow), Kuja (Mars), Shani (Saturn), Nripachakra (King's Chart)</vocab>

<summary>This page details the specific components of the Throne Chart—the Parasol, Whisks, and Throne—and explains how the presence of planets like Saturn and Mars in these positions predicts a king's death or success. It identifies specific constellations that form the "Staff of the Parasol" and notes the differing impacts of benefic and malefic planetary combinations.</summary>

This specific diagram is used to predict outcomes for health, social relationships, and royal stability based on planetary transits through three specific "channels" or paths.</meta>

### The Interpretation of the Three-Channel Chart

One should draw the diagram and, in this **Three-Channel Chart** <term>original: Trinadi Chakra; a divinatory grid used in Jyotisha to categorize constellations into three vertical or horizontal "nerves" or channels</term>, one should declare the results based on the positions of the planets. 

If a channel is occupied by the **Sun** <note>original: Ravi</note> and **Mars** <note>original: Bhauma</note>, or is associated with **Saturn** <note>original: Shani</note> and **Rahu** <term>the North Node of the Moon, considered a shadow planet</term>, that channel should be understood as "afflicted." In such a case, death will occur without a doubt. 

Where two constellations <note>original: rikshadvayam</note> are free from these malefic influences, there may be a confluence of diseases, yet there is also the destruction of obstacles. However, one should also predict separation from kinsmen and distress for the king <note>original: nrupopsargah; a term implying political unrest or a physical ailment affecting the ruler</note>. 

If the channel is joined by **benefic planets** <note>original: saumya-yukta; planets like Jupiter, Venus, or a waxing Moon</note>, one should predict happiness and prosperity. 

When the indicators for a **Master and a Servant** <note>referring to a social or professional hierarchy</note> are situated within the same channel, there is the gain of wealth and a great affection between the two. Likewise, this applies to the friendship between scholars and merchants, and to the peace of the village, the country, and the city.

In this context, if a **Husband and Wife** <note>original: bharya-pati</note> are found situated in a single channel, one should avoid this arrangement. It quickly destroys the household <note>or the 'minister/counselor', as the OCR 'mantiram' could be 'mandiram' (house) or 'mantrin' (minister)</note>; therefore, O Dear One <note>original: priye; addressing the Goddess Parvati</note>, one should abandon such a match.

<summary>This page describes the predictive use of the Trinadi Chakra, warning that malefic planets like Saturn and Mars in the same "channel" signify death or royal distress, while the placement of masters, servants, or spouses in these channels dictates their social and domestic compatibility.</summary>

<keywords>Trinadi Chakra, Jyotisha, Vedic Astrology, planetary omens, relationship compatibility, royal distress, Sun, Mars, Saturn, Rahu</keywords>

If located in the middle, disease arises; at the ends, there is victory. In the middle of the bow-string <note>original: guna; refers both to the string of the bow and the qualities of the celestial alignment</note>, there is passion or conflict; in the middle of the bow itself, there is a loss of wealth. At the top of the bow, victory comes quickly; when aligned with the strings, it is joined with profit. 

Conflict or death is indicated if the placement is joined with malefic planets <term>Papagraha: "cruel planets" like Saturn or Mars that traditionally portend difficulty</term>. O Parvati <note>The text addresses the Goddess, indicating this is a revealed teaching from Shiva</note>, this is especially true in those cases. One should instead predict favorable results when there is a benefic conjunction <term>Saumya-yoga: an auspicious alignment with "gentle" planets like Jupiter or Venus</term>. One should state the final result based on which is stronger: the conjunction of the auspicious or the inauspicious. 

This brief system of characteristics specified in the scriptures should be used to examine the dispelling of good and evil through the wheel in the darkness. 

<margin>Source 6</margin>

Regarding the lunar mansions <note>original: bhani; referring to the Nakshatras</note>: one should write down the mansions starting from Krittika <note>The Pleiades; traditionally the first star of the zodiac in older Vedic systems</note> and those belonging to the Sun. At intervals of five, two, ten, and eleven...

In honor of the **Bow Wheel**, the following times are determined by the lunar days:
* The first day <note>original: Pratipat; the first day of the lunar fortnight</note> is the Time of Sorrow.
* The second day brings death and decay.
* The third day is the Time of Victory.
* The fourth day causes fear.
* On the fifth day, Joy <note>original: Nanda; a term for auspicious dates</note> should be known.
* The sixth day is a time of destruction.
* The seventh day is sorrow...

From that, gain, joy, and contentment [arise]. There are five-fold times...
The Rudras <note>the number eleven, associated with the forms of the god Shiva</note> should be multiplied by three, and the lunar day <term>tithi: a day in the lunar month</term> should be multiplied by two. At the beginning of the month, using the measure of the time-stick <term>velā-daṇḍa: a gnomon or tool used to measure time by shadows</term>, 
one should take the sum. The time [is calculated] on the day of the Sun’s transit <term>saṅkrānti: the moment the sun enters a new zodiac sign</term>.
Having doubled the time-units <term>ghaṭikā: a unit of 24 minutes</term> elapsed since the month of Chaitra <note>original: caitrādi; the first month of the Hindu lunar calendar, typically March–April</note>, one should combine them with the past days. 
Divide that total by seven; the remainder indicates the specific day-type.
The results are said to be: the Day of Prosperity <note>original: Śrī-dina</note>, the Day of Quarrel, the Day of the Journey, 
the Day of the Thorn of Death <note>original: Kālakantaka; a highly inauspicious astrological period</note>, the Day of Virtue <note>original: Dharma-dina</note>, [the sixth is not named here], and the seventh is the Joy of Victory <note>original: Vijayanandana</note>. 
The lunar day should be doubled and 
combined with the numerical value of the letters of the name <note>original: nāmākṣara; a common practice in Indian numerology where names are assigned numbers</note>. One should then take the remainder after dividing by three. 
A remainder of zero is auspicious and 
produces gain for oneself. A remainder of one is also auspicious, O Goddess <note>the text addresses a female deity, typical of the Tantric or Agama style of instructional manuals</note>. That which results in zero is auspicious. 
In the Seven-Spoke Wheel <term>śalākā-cakra: a divinatory diagram used to map planetary positions</term>, there are twenty-seven stars <term>nakṣatras: lunar mansions</term>. 
Where the eclipse-node Rahu <note>the shadow planet believed to "swallow" the sun and moon</note> is situated, that is known as his "mouth," and by that [the reading] is distinguished. 
The constellations <term>ṛkṣāṇi</term> consumed by Rahu... <note>text breaks off</note>

The thirteenth <term>tithi: lunar day</term>, and the twenty-sixth <note>original: dvātrayodaśyā, literally "two thirteens"</note>, are to be known as "deadly" when they are aspected by Rahu <note>the shadow planet or North Node of the Moon, associated with eclipses and misfortune</note>. If the Moon has reached the Sector of Life <note>original: jīva-pakṣe; a favorable astrological division</note> while the Sun is in the Position of Death <term>mṛtyu-pada: an inauspicious astrological location</term>, one should consider that an auspicious time; for a journey undertaken then, one shall obtain wealth. 

However, when the Sun is in the Sector of Life and the Moon is in the Position of Death, then travel will result in loss; there is no need for further deliberation on the matter. 

For the Moon, the birth-constellation <term>nakshatra: one of 27 lunar mansions</term>, the eighth [lunar day], as well as the tenth and the fifteenth, and the twenty-second [constellations]—this group of constellations is known as the "Death of the Moon." 

For the Sun, the birth-constellation, the eighth [lunar day], the tenth, the twenty-first, and the twenty-seventh constellations—these are known as the "Death of the Sun." Without the protection of the Trident <note>original: triśūla; a specific protective astrological formation</note>, death occurs in the middle of the "Eight of Shiva" <note>original: haraṣṭake</note>. 

Profit, victory, and happiness are attained when the Moon resides in the "Womb" <note>original: candra-garbhe; a specific internal sector of a divinatory diagram</note>. With the Moon at the sixteenth constellation, the Sun at the twelfth from the Moon, and Fire <note>the deity Agni or a specific astrological point</note> at the tenth from the Sun—one who understands this "Piercing of Time" <term>kāla-vedha: a technical method of calculating how planetary positions "pierce" or obstruct specific times</term> truly lives.

It concludes that understanding "Kala-vedha" (the piercing of time) allows a practitioner to ensure success in travel and survival.</summary>
