## **I shall now describe the Sun-Wheel, which brings victory in all battles.**

Upon three arrows <note>original: śara</note>—specifically at their tips—and with four spears <term>kunta: a type of barbed lance</term> placed in the corners. There are six shafts <note>original: kāṇḍa</note> running South to North; the Sun is first positioned to the right. 

In the momentum of the arrows <note>original: puṃvegeṣu; possibly referring to the points of force</note>, there will be disease; in the spears, there will be death. If the Sun is located in the shafts on the right side, one's own army shall face defeat. In the tips <note>original: phalageṣu; literally the "fruit" or blades of the weapons</note>, there is the suffering of hunger. 

However, in the left shafts and within the "three cities" <note>likely referring to three protected central sectors of the diagram</note>, victory is achieved; in all tasks and in the heat of combat, it shall be successful.

**Thus having established the Sun-Wheel, the procedure for the Battle-Wheel <note>original: yuddha-cakra</note> is as follows:**

One should draw three tridents <term>śūla: the three-pronged spear associated with Shiva</term> in the East. In the corners, one should draw four crescent-headed arrows <note>original: bhalla; a specialized heavy arrow with a semi-circular blade</note>. One should then draw the four arrows and the Sun-Wheels in the North and South. 

The results of this diagram are:
*   In the tridents, there will be death.
*   In the bases <note>original: mūla</note> of the weapons, there is victory in battle.
*   In the "diseased" sectors, death occurs.
*   In the sectors of "gain," death is certain. <note>This likely refers to a specific paradox where over-extension for "gain" leads to a fatal trap.</note>
*   In the corners, one should know there is victory, the attainment of all success, and the arrival of wealth.

One should calculate the **Solar Transit** <note>original: Saṅkrānti; the moment the sun moves into a new zodiac sign</note> using the traditional methods. Through the rule of three and six, one determines the fate of kings and their honor among other rulers. This calculation reveals whether one will experience a **loss of position** <note>original: sthānacyutiḥ</note> or an **increase of position** <note>original: sthānavivardhane</note>. 

Inscribe the eighteen lunar mansions within the three tridents <note>original: tri-saṃśūla; referring to the three-pronged lines in the diagram</note>. Write them in sets of three and six, including the *Krittika* stars. When these are properly drawn, the results are accurately proclaimed through five factors: the people, fear, knowledge, garments, and excellent women. These are the defining characteristics of the **Solar Transit**.

---

-> The Terrible Wheel within the Sun-Wheel <-
<note>original: Sūryacakre Ugracakraṃ. An "Ugra" wheel is often used to predict harsh or intense events, such as war or sudden political shifts.</note>

**Left labels:** Revati, Uttara Bhadrapada, Purva Bhadrapada, Shatabhisha, Krittika <note>original: kānd</note>, Dhanishtha, Shravana, [unclear], Kumbha <note>Aquarius</note>.

<term>Sankranti</term> (Solar Transit): The moment the Sun enters a new zodiac sign, used in astrology to predict the month's influence on society and leadership.
<term>Ugra Chakra</term> (Terrible/Powerful Wheel): A specific diagnostic grid used to identify "terrible" or "harsh" influences, often involving the loss of power or the onset of conflict.
<term>Sthanacyuti</term> (Loss of position): A specific astrological prediction indicating a demotion, exile, or loss of professional standing.

# **The Moon Wheel, O Goddess, positioned according to the directions**

Placed in the center of the Saturn Circle <term>Shani Mandala</term> <note>an astrological diagram used to map the influence of Saturn</note>, one should calculate the movements of the Sun and the other planets. If the planets are placed in the upper lines of the diagram, it signifies death. 

Sickness is indicated in the middle staff <term>Danda</term> <note>original: madhya-dande; referring to the central vertical axis of the grid</note>, but in the other staffs, happiness is observed everywhere. Alternatively, one should write three sections in the East and North, then three, then four, and three roots in the corners, in sequence. These should be written starting from the Sun's birth star.

The wise should examine the Moon in that diagram. If it falls in the Eastern points, there will be illness; in the Southern points, there will be blood loss or injury <note>original: rudhira-kshuti; literally a "discharge of blood"</note>; in the Northern points, when the Moon is present, auspiciousness is seen everywhere. If the Moon is positioned in the center, one attains kingship; if it is on the spear-points <term>Shulage</term> <note>referring to the sharp outer points of a trident-like diagram</note>, death is certain. A malefic Moon is always harmful to the King, whereas a benefic Moon always brings prosperity. Therefore, one should examine it carefully.

**Now I shall describe the Mars Wheel <term>Kuja Chakra</term>. It is drawn in the shape of a lion by the wise, with three stars in each [part] of the wheel...**

<summary>This page provides instructions for constructing and interpreting the Moon Wheel, a grid-based tool for predicting health, success, and the fate of rulers. It concludes by introducing the Mars Wheel, which is traditionally drawn in the shape of a lion.</summary>
<keywords>Moon Wheel, Chandra Chakra, Mars Wheel, Kuja Chakra, divination, astrology, Saturn Circle, kingship, medical astrology</keywords>

One should write [the stars] on the body of the diagram in sequence, starting from the Sun’s birth star <term>Suryarksha</term> <note>the specific lunar mansion or Nakshatra where the Sun was positioned at birth</note>. In all matters, and specifically regarding the bodies of the planets, one should examine the twenty-seven <term>Saptavimshati</term> <note>referring to the 27 Nakshatras or lunar mansions of Vedic astrology</note> constellations. These should be considered in relation to the head, the neck, the back, the secret parts <note>original: guhye; referring here to the anus or private regions of the symbolic lion</note>, and the very tips of the diagram. 

Consider the positions of Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> and Mercury <term>Saumya</term> <note>literally "the son of the Moon"</note> along with Mars <term>Bhauma</term> <note>literally "son of the Earth"</note>. If Rahu is positioned there, one should understand it signifies death. If the Sun is present, it causes fever and confusion. If Saturn <term>Saura</term> is present, there is destruction of work and failure in all endeavors on every side. 

If Mars <term>Mahija</term> <note>another name for Mars, "Earth-born"</note> is located in the head, one finds happiness and is devoted to all duties. In the neck, there occurs disease. On the back, there is the attainment of all wealth and success. In the anus, however, all work becomes fruitless. In the tail, there is fear at the front of battle <term>Ranagre</term>.

When Mars is positioned in the foot, the successful fruit of all endeavors arises. O Beautiful One <note>original: sudari/sundari; a vocative address to the Goddess to whom the text is spoken</note>, the Mars Wheel <term>Kuja Chakra</term> can produce a multitude of evils in one's actions if poorly placed. Thus has been described the Mars Wheel, which brings complete conviction in all matters.

**Now I shall describe the Mercury Wheel <term>Budha Chakra</term>.**

At the time of departure <term>prasthāna</term> <note>referring to setting out on a journey, a military expedition, or a major life event</note>, to ensure a successful outcome, one should write [the stars] in whichever positions they fall upon the **head of the deity**, O dear one. These are written starting from the **forehead**, then the eyes, the nose, the mouth, the chin, and the throat;
upon the **shoulders**, the arms, and the hands; the chest, the sides, and the back; the **navel**, the genitals, and the anus; and likewise upon the **feet**.
By writing the form in this sequence, the following outcomes are obtained: In the
**forehead**, there is grief and burning distress. In the **eyes**, a pleasant sight. In the **ears**, there is enmity with kinsmen. In the
**nose**, there is the arrival of happiness. In the **mouth**, there is a mixture of food
and drink. In the **chin**, there is the highest affection. In the **throat**, 
there is the attainment of a wife and a son. In the **shoulders**, one should
predict gain. In the **arms**, one should know the arrival of all provisions <note>original: "sarvastrāgamam," which can imply the acquisition of weapons, tools, or general goods</note>.
In the **hands**, there is battle and the advance of enemies. In the **chest**, there
would be the destruction of affection. In the **sides**, predict disease...

# The Wheel of Mercury for Departures

Upon **departure**, the **results** are **borne** in this **wheel** of **Mercury** <note>original: "Budha"</note>.

Through Mercury...

| Position | Result |
| :--- | :--- |
| On the Directions | [Inferred result based on context] |
| On the Back | Fear of the royal court <note>original: "rājakulāt-trāsaḥ"; refers to legal trouble or problems with local authorities</note> |
| At the Navel | Union shall occur; the birth of a son shall be. |
| At the Genitals or Anus | Predict death. <term>Om</term> <note>A sacred syllable marking the end of a specific instruction</note> |
| At the Feet | There is suffering; great [misfortune]. |

<insert>
When malefic planets <note>original: "pāpāḥ"</note> are situated in a house that is usually auspicious, they act in the opposite way. For the sake of auspiciousness, when planets move even slightly into a favorable position, Oh Beautiful One <note>Addressing the goddess, likely Parvati</note>, the person is freed from infamy, though disease may still afflict the man...
</insert>

Even in inauspicious times, if the position is consumed by Rahu <note>The shadow planet responsible for eclipses, often signaling sudden disaster</note>, death will occur—there is no doubt about this. If joined by auspicious planets, there is safety, health <note>original: "ārogyaṃ"</note>, happiness, and prosperity.

Thus it has been told. What else do you wish to ask?

It also includes a clarifying note on how malefic planets and the node Rahu can reverse or intensify these effects.</summary>

<note>original abbreviations: yu. ja. na. 66. This refers to the *Yuddhajayanirnaya*, a manual on "Determining Victory in Battle."</note>

...shape of a bird, O Beloved <note>original: "priye." The text continues to address Parvati.</note>. For the wheel of Venus <note>original: "Bhārgava," a name for the planet Venus</note>, three constellations <term>Nakshatras: Lunar mansions of the zodiac</term> are placed in the beak.

On the two wings, one should place six constellations (three on each side). Three are placed in the tail, and six in the feet. One should carefully write these constellations in their designated places.

If the planet or moon is positioned in the **beak**, there will be the **destruction of the enemy, victory, and the attainment of great fame.**

However, if it is at the **tip of the crest** <note>original: "śikhāgre"</note>, it signifies the **breaking of the army** <note>meaning a rout or total defeat</note>. 

If the **wings** are occupied by auspicious planets, it is especially favorable for conquering other lands. If malefic planets <note>original: "pāpaiḥ," referring to planets like Mars or Saturn that portend difficulty</note> are situated in the Ascendant <term>Lagna: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</term>, the commander of the cavalry will be affected. 

With the presence of auspicious planets, there is the destruction of the enemy. Conversely, there is a lack of glory if they are absent. If malefic or even auspicious planets are in the **beak or the tail**, victory may still occur. However, if they are in the **feet**, whether auspicious or malefic, there will be a **loss of honor in battle.**

<summary>This page details the construction of the bird-shaped "Wheel of Venus" (Bhargava-cakra) for military divination, mapping constellations to the bird's beak, wings, tail, and feet to predict victory or defeat.</summary>

</margin>

One should consider the results <note>original: "phala"</note> with a calm mind, regarding the three levels of the fort <note>original: "kota-traya"</note> defined by the inner, middle, and outer lines. These divisions are considered collectively.

<insert>situated</insert>

The results for the village or town <note>original: "gramam"</note> are said to be of three kinds. If **benefic planets** <term>Shubha-graha: Auspicious celestial bodies like Jupiter, Venus, and Mercury that bring positive influence</term> are positioned in the middle, there will be growth, victory, and happiness. If **malefic planets** <term>Papa-graha: Inauspicious bodies like Mars, Saturn, or Rahu that signify conflict or loss</term> are in the middle, there will be loss, fear of thieves, and enemies. 

When "cruel" planets are in the "womb" <note>the innermost center of the diagram</note> of the city, they act as agents of destruction. If the city is surrounded by "fire" <note>likely referring to the placement of the Sun or Mars</note>, there will be military conflict and death—of this, there is no doubt. It is certain that when cruel planets are in the center and also occupy the outer areas, the city is in peril. 

If benefics are in the middle and cruel planets are coming from the outside, it is called a "difficult fort" <note>original: "durgam," implying a state of siege or hardship</note>. The specific directions are interpreted as follows:

*   **Northeast:** Fear of kings.
*   **East:** Enmity and hostility.
*   **Southeast:** Fear of kings, yet also the destruction of enemies and the gaining of profit.
*   **Southwest:** The slaughter or downfall of kings.
*   **West:** Danger or trouble for the king's wife.
*   **Northwest:** The destruction of the fort itself.
*   **North:** The destruction of servants or attendants.

This is the traditional judgment based on the planets. 

### **The Nature of the Planets**

The **Sun** <note>original: "Ravi"</note>, **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note>, **Saturn** <note>original: "Shani"</note>, and **Rahu** <term>Rahu: The North Node of the Moon, considered a "shadow planet" and a powerful malefic in Vedic astrology</term>—along with a waning or afflicted Moon—are considered malefic. One must carefully determine the presence of **benefic planets** in the center of the city to ensure success and happiness.

They <note>referring to the malefic planets mentioned previously</note> always bring the fear of thieves and fire. However, if **Venus, the Moon, Mercury, and Jupiter** <note>original: "śukra-indu-jña-guru"</note> are positioned in the middle, growth and prosperity should be known to occur.

There is a promise of growth for the town starting from its founding; if the **Sun** <note>original: "sūrya"</note> is in the fifth position from the village's birth star, one should know that the village will be truly prosperous, O fair-faced one <note>a traditional poetic address to the reader or a deity, common in astrological dialogues</note>. 

According to the experts of the **Village Wheel** <term>Gramachakra: A specialized astrological diagram used to predict the fate of a town or settlement based on its name or founding time</term>, if the Sun is in the fifth, twentieth, or tenth positions, and for the village, O beautiful woman, planets are in the fourth, eleventh, tenth, or fifth **lunar mansions** <note>original: "rksha"</note>... 

When **Rahu**, **Saturn**, the **Sun**, **Mars**, **Mercury**, the **Moon**, and **Ketu** <note>these are the celestial bodies of the Sanskrit zodiac</note> are positioned in a certain way, the town is greatly oppressed. 

To calculate the specific timing of these events, use the following method:

1.  Take the number associated with the village’s **lunar mansion** <term>Nakshatra: The specific star constellation the Moon occupied at the town's founding</term>.
2.  Add the number for the **zodiac sign** <note>original: "rashi"</note> and the current sign of the Moon.
3.  Likewise, add the current **lunar day** <term>Tithi: One of thirty divisions of a lunar month</term> and the **day of the week** <term>Vara: The seven days named after the planets</term>. 

Combine these numbers and divide the total by three. If the remainder is **one**, the influence is "Entered" <note>suggesting an incoming force or threat</note>. If the remainder is **two**, it is "Stationed" <note>implying a stable or persisting condition</note>. If the remainder is **zero**, it shall be... <note>The text likely continued on the next page to describe the "Exit" or "Outer" state</note>.
